These Rio card stories are very strong for **post-origin Rio**: not the desperate “I must win or everything collapses” Rio, but the older LizNoir center who has learned to live inside affection, rivalry, embarrassment, softness, professional ambition, and ordinary adult exhaustion without losing her edge.

## Overall read on this Rio batch

The through-line is that Rio is an **adult idol whose pride has become livable**. Earlier Rio is defined by necessity: protect the café, earn money, surpass Mana, prove LizNoir’s perfection, refuse weakness. These stories show what happens after that pressure has been socialized into relationships. She is still competitive, still elegant, still exacting, still easily provoked by rankings or rivals, but she can now laugh, be cared for, be teased, drink too much, admit uncertainty, ask for rest, and let other people surprise her.

A useful thesis for this batch:

**Rio’s mature identity is built around the reconciliation of pride and dependence: she still wants to stand at the center and take LizNoir to the top, but she no longer has to pretend that she can do it without being loved, interrupted, corrected, teased, supported, or seen.**

## 001 — “莉央のわがまま” / Rio’s Selfish Wish

card\_rio\_001\_st-card-rio-05-bir…

### Summary

This birthday story begins with Rio being asked what she wants for a birthday feature. Her “selfish wish” is not jewelry, luxury, or attention, but **“今まで食べたことのないパンケーキ”** — “a pancake I’ve never eaten before.” Aoi immediately understands that this is harder than it sounds because Rio has eaten through much of Tokyo’s pancake scene and does not document all of it publicly. He recruits Rei, based on the absurd principle **“ダンスがうまい人は料理がうまい”** — “people good at dancing are good at cooking.” Their attempts become chaotic, but Aoi eventually turns the request into a live birthday surprise, presenting Rio with a handmade pancake after her performance.

### What it reveals about Rio

This is a deceptively important Rio story because it shows how modest her desires are when she is not performing command. Her “わがまま” / “selfishness” is extremely Rio-like: practical, food-centered, restrained, and difficult only because she has quietly built a whole private life of pancake exploration. She is not asking to be adored in grandiose fashion. She wants a new sensory experience, a tiny unknown pleasure, something that belongs to her offstage self.

It also reinforces Rio’s **food memory** as a major character axis. Pancakes are not random moe flavor; they tie back to her café upbringing, her father’s cooking, her idea of being cared for, and her ability to experience happiness through taste. The fact that Aoi remembers Rio’s joy more than the actual menu details is very Aoi, but it also says something about Rio: when she is eating something she loves, her guardedness drops enough that others remember the expression.

### Relationship contribution

Aoi’s role is crucial. He does not solve the request elegantly; he solves it in an Aoi way — wandering logic, bodily intuition, strange confidence, sincerity masked as absurdity. But he understands the emotional shape of Rio’s desire. His surprise says: “I know you well enough to know that the perfect gift is not a thing, but an experience only we can create.” Rei’s participation extends Rio’s social world beyond LizNoir, showing how respected and cared-for she is across Hoshimi Pro.

### Key phrasing

**“莉央のわがまま”** — “Rio’s selfishness / whim.” The title is affectionate irony. Rio’s “selfishness” is almost too reasonable, which makes the others’ effort feel warmer.

**“今まで食べたことのないパンケーキ”** — “a pancake I’ve never eaten before.” This is Rio’s adult version of fantasy: not extravagance, but novelty, craftsmanship, taste, and personal memory.

**“ダンスがうまい人は料理がうまい”** — “people good at dancing are good at cooking.” Aoi’s nonsense logic, but thematically useful: in his mind, embodied sensitivity transfers across arts.

### Classification

**Important.** Not the single most central Rio text, but highly useful for her private desires, Aoi’s understanding of her, her café/food motif, and the way love for Rio often appears through practical effort rather than sentimental speeches.

---

## 002 — “慰安旅行 in沖縄” / Company Retreat in Okinawa

card\_rio\_002\_st-card-rio-05-bir…

### Summary

Rio, Aoi, Fran, Nagisa, and Mei go on a birthday-adjacent company retreat to Okinawa, with the manager accompanying them after Satomi gets sick. Rio immediately falls into caretaker mode: managing free spirits, tidying the room, keeping track of Aoi and Fran, worrying about everyone else’s behavior, and acting like the responsible adult. The manager notices and arranges a night out at an Okinawan izakaya to thank her. Rio loosens up, complains honestly, drinks too much, admits she also wanted to be freer, and ends up asking for one more drink on the beach while enjoying a rare “nothing” moment. The next day, both Rio and the manager are hungover.

### What it reveals about Rio

This is one of the best “adult Rio” stories because it shows the cost of her competence. Rio does not merely act responsible; she reflexively becomes responsible. Even on a慰安旅行 / “comfort trip” meant to let her rest, she turns into the person who manages everyone else’s freedom. Her exasperation is comic, but it has pathos: Rio’s maturity can become a trap. Because she is reliable, others relax around her; because others relax around her, she cannot relax.

The story also gives Rio a rare chance to voice something almost childish: **“私だってたまにはちょっとくらい羽目を外して遊んだっていいと思わない？”** — “Don’t you think even I should be allowed to cut loose a little once in a while?” That line matters. Rio is aware that her own image traps her. She wants permission to stop being the leader, older sister, chaperone, and sensible one.

### Relationship contribution

The manager’s role here is not romantic in a simple way, but it is intimate. He recognizes that Rio’s exhaustion is invisible to most people because she disguises it as authority. He does not give her a grand present; he gives her a controlled space where she can complain, drink, and sit quietly by the sea. For Rio, being cared for means someone notices that she is always caring for others.

The story also places Rio beside Fran as another adult woman in the cast. Fran is self-interested, stylish, and profit-sensitive; Rio is responsible, sharp, and service-minded. Their contrast makes Rio’s “adultness” feel less generic: she is not simply older; she is **burdened by a habit of stewardship**.

### Key phrasing

**“慰安旅行”** — “comfort trip / company retreat.” The irony is that Rio initially experiences the trip as more labor.

**“私のお願いを聞いてくれる……？”** — “Will you listen to my request…?” The phrase is framed with unusual softness, then humorously undercut by her wanting more alcohol. Even so, the softness is real: Rio is testing whether she can ask for indulgence.

**“何もしない時間”** — “time doing nothing.” This is one of the most important ideas in the story. Rio needs not just rest, but permission to exist without utility.

### Classification

**Core for mature Rio.** This is essential for understanding Rio’s adult fatigue, caretaker instincts, relationship with the manager, and the softer self she usually hides behind discipline.

## 003 — “空き巣に入られてるじゃない” / “You’ve Been Burgled, Haven’t You?”

card\_rio\_003\_st-card-rio-05-bir…

### Summary

On Rio’s birthday, she receives birthday messages from others and has three separate encounters. First, Fran takes her out for sweets, then comically fails to pay because she forgot her wallet. Rio follows her home and sees Fran’s messy room, joking that it looks like a burglary scene. She also negotiates for one of Fran’s black jackets, recognizing its style value. Second, Kokoro secretly records a birthday video letter thanking Rio for leading LizNoir and declaring that Rio is a proper leader “for LizNoir,” even if Kokoro will someday take the center. Third, Ai panics over not having prepared a gift, so Rio turns the moment into cooking together. They make omurice, and Rio eventually recreates a taste close to her father’s cooking, feeling that she has become “one more step” adult.

### What it reveals about Rio

This story is a compact map of Rio’s current social identity.

With Fran, Rio is witty, sharp, and surprisingly playful. She can spar with another adult woman without becoming hostile. Her eye for the black jacket also reinforces her aesthetic intelligence: Rio understands black not as absence, but as selection, texture, and image. That matters for LizNoir, whose black-lily identity depends on elegance rather than flat darkness.

With Kokoro, Rio receives one of the clearest confirmations of her leadership. Kokoro’s line that Rio is **“『リズノワにとって』立派なリーダー”** — “a fine leader for LizNoir” — is carefully phrased. It does not mean Rio is a perfect universal leader. It means her exact mix of strictness, pride, vulnerability, and responsibility is what LizNoir needs. Kokoro also challenges her by promising to take the center someday, which is precisely the kind of affection Rio can accept: love expressed through rivalry.

With Ai, the emotional core comes through food and inheritance. Rio does not demand a present; she turns Ai’s anxiety into shared labor. When the taste resembles her father’s, the birthday becomes less about receiving and more about recovering memory. The line **“大人になれた気がするわ”** — “I feel like I’ve become a little more adult” — is not just about age. It is about integrating childhood loss without being ruled by it.

### Relationship contribution

Kokoro and Ai are no longer intruders into the “real” LizNoir. They are now Rio’s juniors, challengers, and emotional witnesses. Kokoro gives Rio confidence; Ai gives Rio a route back to gentleness. Fran, meanwhile, expands Rio’s peer network and shows that Rio can be mischievous with people outside her own unit.

### Key phrasing

**“空き巣に入られてるじゃない”** — “You’ve been burgled, haven’t you?” Rio’s line is blunt, domestic, and funny. It shows how her severity can become comedy when she is relaxed.

**“『リズノワにとって』立派なリーダー”** — “a fine leader for LizNoir.” This is one of the best leadership formulations for Rio: contextual, earned, not abstract.

**“お父さんの味そっくり”** — “it tastes just like Dad’s.” Food becomes memory, family, and emotional continuity.

**“大人になれた気がするわ”** — “I feel like I’ve become a little more adult.” A quiet statement of mourning transformed into growth.

### Classification

**Core.** This is one of the best all-around Rio character files in the batch because it triangulates her through Fran, Kokoro, Ai, fashion, leadership, food memory, and father-loss.

---

## 004 — “敗北を味わわせてあげる” / “I’ll Let Them Taste Defeat”

card\_rio\_004\_st-card-rio-05-cas…

### Summary

Rio is offered a role as the heroine in a romance game themed around “adult love.” She initially doubts that she fits the image of romance, because she has little dating experience: middle school was track and student council, high school was idol work. Her motivation changes after she falls out of an “idol you’d want as a bride” ranking and is teased by Kokoro. She recruits Rui, who also fell out of the ranking, and resolves to make the whole world want to marry them. When working on the game questionnaire, Rio first describes a high-school rivalry romance, only to be told the game requires adult romance. The manager helps her reimagine herself as a workaholic office woman, feared by others but secretly capable of affection. Later, Rio’s favorite scene — gently stroking a sleeping lover’s head — is revealed to have been inspired by watching the manager sleep at his desk and unconsciously murmuring support for her.

### What it reveals about Rio

This file is essential for Rio’s emotional and romantic imagination. Rio claims she has no connection to romance, but the story proves the opposite: she has a vivid romantic inner life, just one shaped by rivalry, work, trust, and everyday care rather than obvious flirtation.

Her first instinct is **rival romance**: two people who clash, compete, respect each other, walk home together, quarrel, share dreams, and gradually wonder whether the feeling is friendship or love. That is extremely Rio. Her ideal intimacy is not passive adoration; it is mutual recognition through struggle. Love, for Rio, begins when another person can stand beside her without being crushed by her standards.

When forced into “adult love,” she imagines office work, secret trust, and the intimacy of being relied upon by a junior. Again, this is not accidental: Rio’s own adult identity is built around overwork, leadership, and responsibility. She sees affection emerging from shared labor.

The final sleeping-headpat scene is more revealing than any confession. Rio understands love through the moment when someone exhausted is still thinking of another person. She is moved by the manager’s sleeptalking because it suggests that care for her exists below conscious duty. His support is not only professional performance; it leaks out even when he is asleep.

### Relationship contribution

This is one of the most manager-intimate Rio stories. It does not need explicit confession to be emotionally charged. Rio becomes embarrassed by being understood, then wants to make the manager feel what she feels: **“私ばかりドキドキさせられて不公平”** — “It’s unfair that I’m the only one being made to feel flustered.” That is classic Rio: vulnerable feeling converted into competitive retaliation.

It also makes Kokoro’s teasing structurally important. Kokoro provokes Rio into action, and Rio’s pride becomes a route to self-exploration. Rui’s inclusion is also useful: Rio finds solidarity with another stoic, romantically awkward, high-performing leader.

### Key phrasing

**“敗北を味わわせてあげる”** — “I’ll let them taste defeat.” Rio turns romantic appeal into combat language. She wants players not merely to like her, but to be defeated by her charm.

**“競い合い共に切磋琢磨するライバルとの恋愛”** — “romance with a rival who competes and mutually improves with you.” This may be the cleanest description of Rio’s ideal intimacy.

**“年上残念美人”** — “older unfortunate beauty / beautiful but hopeless older woman.” She recognizes the archetype fits and resents it fitting.

**“愛おしいと思う瞬間”** — “the moment you feel someone is dear.” The answer becomes ordinary care, not grand passion.

### Classification

**Core.** This is indispensable for Rio’s inner emotional world, her awkwardness around romance, her competitiveness, her manager relationship, and the translation of pride into tenderness.

---

## 005 — “大人げない自分” / Her Immature Self

card\_rio\_005\_st-card-rio-05-fes…

### Summary

After New Year, Rio pushes LizNoir through intense training before a live battle against a rising BanPro group, in the same venue as the NEXT VENUS Grand Prix. The manager intervenes because Kokoro and Ai are exhausted. Rio admits that she is anxious and that when she becomes heated, her **“大人げない自分”** — immature self — comes out. Later, she drinks with the manager and talks about how much she has changed since joining Hoshimi Pro. She asks the uncomfortable question: who does the manager want to stand at the top in the end? On battle day, Rio is calm but powerful; she declares that LizNoir will charm even rivals and win perfectly. After a dominant victory, she thanks the audience and reframes their success not as a miracle, but as **“必然”** — inevitability created by effort and support.

### What it reveals about Rio

This is one of the strongest leadership-development stories for Rio. The first part shows regression: old pressure, old venue, old BanPro associations, old defeat-memory. Rio’s trauma is not gone. The NEXT VENUS venue and BanPro opponent awaken the Rio who believes that loosening her grip will invite collapse.

But the key difference is that she can now recognize the pattern. She says, in effect, “I know I am not that old self anymore, but anxiety brings her back.” That self-awareness is a major mark of growth. Rio does not need the manager to tell her she is wrong; she needs him to stop her before her anxiety becomes a group atmosphere.

The bar conversation is equally important because it shows Rio as an adult who can hold the manager’s burdens. She notices his pressure and gives him permission to complain. Her question — **“誰に頂点に立ってほしいのかしら？”** / “Who do you want to stand at the top in the end?” — cuts to the central contradiction of managing multiple rival groups. Rio is mature enough to retract it, but proud enough to want the answer.

By the final performance, she has rebalanced: not frantic, not cold, but centered. Her ideal victory is no longer “overwhelm because we must prove ourselves or die.” It is **“ライバルさえ魅了する”** — “to charm even our rivals.” That is a huge refinement of LizNoir’s philosophy. Defeat is still defeat, but the highest form of defeat is aesthetic conversion: even the opponent must recognize the beauty of LizNoir.

### Relationship contribution

The manager becomes Rio’s necessary external brake. He does not extinguish her fire; he helps regulate it. Rio explicitly asks whether he can keep stopping her when she overheats. That shows trust. Rio does not want to become mild. She wants someone strong and honest enough to keep her intensity useful.

Kokoro and Ai’s exhaustion matters too. Rio’s growth is measured by whether she can lead juniors without burning them. The story acknowledges that Rio’s standards are inspiring and dangerous.

### Key phrasing

**“大人げない自分”** — “my immature self.” Rio’s pride includes shame at emotional regression.

**“誰に頂点に立ってほしいのかしら？”** — “Who do you want to stand at the top?” A sharp articulation of Hoshimi Pro’s internal rivalry problem.

**“ライバルさえ魅了する”** — “to charm even rivals.” A mature LizNoir thesis: victory as aesthetic conquest.

**“奇跡よりずっと凄い必然”** — “an inevitability far greater than a miracle.” This is very Rio. She respects miracle, but her pride belongs to effort, support, and earned causality.

### Classification

**Core.** This is central for Rio as leader, center, rival, adult confidante, and reformed-but-not-softened competitor.

---

## 006 — “莉央への密着インタビュー” / A Close Interview with Rio

card\_rio\_006\_st-card-rio-05-fes…

### Summary

LizNoir is selected for a major year-end national music program — the same kind of prestigious stage Mana once appeared on. A documentary-style interview is required, and Rio agrees only if the manager is the one filming and interviewing her. Across the interview, she talks about live performance, her café childhood, her father’s coffee and pancakes, helping at the family shop, her father’s death, the financial threat to the café, and her decision to become an idol to support her mother and protect the shop. She then reflects on Mana as a rival and an object of admiration. In the end, she realizes that what began as a job to protect her family became something to which she has devoted all her youth. Her final message to her past self is: **“歌いなさい、神崎莉央…貪欲にトップを目指しなさい”** — “Sing, Kanzaki Rio… greedily aim for the top.”

### What it reveals about Rio

This is the most explicitly autobiographical file in the batch and probably the most authoritative single Rio self-account among these six. It confirms the full arc:

Rio’s origin is not idol dreaminess. It is family duty. She was a café daughter who expected to continue the family business, loved her parents, helped in the shop, absorbed coffee, cooking, and hospitality from childhood, and only entered entertainment after her father’s death threatened the café’s survival. That gives her ambition its original moral seriousness. She did not become severe because she wanted to look cool. She became severe because failure had material consequences.

But the file also makes clear that this origin is no longer the whole story. The line **“元々は実家の喫茶店を守るために始めた仕事に過ぎなかったのに……いつの間にか、青春全部を捧げてここにいるわ”** — “It began merely as a job to protect my family’s café… and before I knew it, I had devoted all my youth to this” — is the key transformation. Idolhood becomes Rio’s life, not only her instrument.

Mana’s role is also clarified with nuance. Rio says Mana was not a villain; Rio simply rivaled her. Mana was pure, free, and joyful in song, while Rio was driven by the need to win. Mana’s death left Rio unable to resolve the rivalry and caused her to stop singing for a year. Yet the story’s endpoint is not resentment. Mana becomes **“永遠のライバル”** and **“憧れ”** — eternal rival and object of admiration. That combination is central to Rio’s pride: she does not need to diminish Mana to surpass her; she needs to keep singing.

### Relationship contribution

Rio asking the manager to film her is deeply meaningful. She is willing to expose her biography because he is the one behind the camera. The parallel with Mana’s old interview also matters: Rio wants to stand where Mana stood, but not as an imitation. She wants the same structure, the same witness, the same historical stage — then to assert LizNoir’s own flower-like blooming.

The manager’s camera becomes a device of trust. Rio lets herself be recorded not because she likes vulnerability, but because she trusts who is looking.

### Key phrasing

**“ライブとはなんですか？”** — “What is a live performance to you?” Rio answers that it is self-expression and the closest chance to meet fans, linking her cool stage image to gratitude.

**“実家が喫茶店”** — “my family home runs a café.” This is one of her root identifiers.

**“青春全部を捧げて”** — “devoting all my youth.” Rio names idolhood as life expenditure.

**“歌を纏って咲き誇れ！ LizNoir！”** — “Clothe yourselves in song and bloom proudly, LizNoir!” Excellent Rio/LizNoir language: elegance, embodiment, flower imagery, command.

**“トップを目指しなさい”** — “Aim for the top.” Her message to her past self does not comfort the old Rio by telling her to rest. It tells her to keep being Rio — to sing, train, and hunger for the summit.

### Classification

**Core / foundational.** This is indispensable for any full Rio reference document. It links family, café, father, Mana, grief, ambition, LizNoir, manager trust, and Rio’s own retrospective self-understanding.

---

## Synthesis: what these six stories add to Rio

This batch deepens Rio in five major ways.

First, it makes her **domestic and embodied**. Rio is not just black-stage elegance and technical pride. She is coffee, pancakes, omurice sauce, father’s recipes, messy hotel rooms, alcohol, hangovers, quiet beaches, and the tactile act of stroking someone’s hair. Her emotional life is routed through concrete actions.

Second, it shows her as **a leader who can still regress**. “大人げない自分” is the key. Rio’s old severity has not vanished; it returns under pressure. But now she can name it, feel ashamed of it, and let trusted people interrupt it.

Third, it clarifies that Rio’s ideal intimacy is **competitive partnership**. Whether in romance, management, Aoi, Kokoro, Rui, or rivals, she is drawn to people who stand beside her, challenge her, and understand effort. She does not want to be worshiped from below. She wants to be met.

Fourth, it reframes LizNoir’s perfection. Earlier Rio’s perfection could feel defensive: “we must be perfect because anything less is failure.” Mature Rio’s perfection is more generous: “we will charm even rivals; we will turn miracle into inevitability; we will make effort beautiful enough that others must recognize it.”

Fifth, it makes Rio’s adultness emotionally credible. She is older than many idols in the cast not just in age, but in accumulated responsibility: dead father, endangered family business, money pressure, leadership burden, failed rivalry with Mana, lost year, juniors to guide, and a center position she refuses to surrender. Yet she is also allowed to be funny, needy, embarrassed, sentimental, and occasionally ridiculous.

The best compact formulation after these files would be:

**Kanzaki Rio is an idol who began as a daughter trying to protect a café, became a rival trying to defeat a miracle, broke when that miracle disappeared, and matured into a center who understands that pride is not the refusal to need others, but the resolve to keep singing beautifully because others have helped her stand.**

Mature Rio still refuses defeat, but her pride becomes more flexible: she can accept help, take unfamiliar jobs, look ridiculous, admit jealousy, be moved by memory, and still convert all of it into LizNoir’s dignity.**

## 007 — “負けられない理由” / The Reason She Cannot Lose

card\_rio\_007\_st-card-rio-05-fes…

### Summary

This story revolves around the **Grab the BIG4** period and the new outfit tied to LizNoir’s song **「情熱だけで生きてみろ」**. Rio asks the manager to rush a new costume into readiness for the tournament, even though Aoi is absent and the song itself is not ready. She knows the request is unreasonable, but she wants any possible advantage because LizNoir must win. Her reasons are not only personal ambition: as leader, she wants to take everyone higher, and she wants to repay Aoi for waiting for her during her lost year.

The manager cannot get the outfit ready for the first match, but promises to make it possible by the semifinal. Rio promises in return that LizNoir will survive until then. The story later revisits the semifinal against TRINITYAiLE, where Rio worries that using a new outfit while TRINITYAiLE uses older costumes may feel unfair. Once she understands Rui’s side is also making a deliberate “victory” choice, Rio resolves to fight without hesitation. The final part reframes the costume as a symbolic anchor: even before it appeared onstage, the promise around it supported Rio through the tournament. She finally articulates her idol philosophy directly: for her, **idolhood is “battle”** — throwing oneself into struggle to fulfill one’s wish.

### What it reveals about Rio

This is a core Rio file because it shows the difference between **cheap pride** and **true pride**. Rio explicitly says she would throw away her “cheap pride” if doing so helps LizNoir win. That is not humility in the soft sense. It is the opposite: Rio’s pride has become serious enough that she no longer needs to look elegant at every moment. If crawling, pleading, relying on others, or appearing desperate is necessary, she will do it.

The outfit becomes a talisman of mediated support. Rio likes to imagine herself as someone who advances by will, training, and center-stage force, but this story shows that her strength is sustained by a network: the manager, designers, staff, Aoi’s hoped-for return, Kokoro and Ai’s continued effort, and the promise that LizNoir will keep advancing. The “battle” is hers, but she does not fight alone.

It also gives one of the best formulations of Rio’s relationship to Aoi. Aoi is not just an old partner or emotional comfort. He is someone Rio feels she must **repayer**: he waited for her for a year, stayed beside her, and believed in her return. Rio wants victory to become a form of answer to that loyalty.

### Key phrases

**「なりふり構わずあがいてやる」** — “I’ll struggle with no concern for appearances.”
A major Rio line. She is willing to become unsightly if that is what winning honestly requires.

**「私の安いプライドなんて、いくらでも捨ててやるわ」** — “I’ll throw away my cheap pride as much as needed.”
This separates vanity from vocation. Rio’s real pride is not looking unshaken; it is refusing to stop.

**「私にとっては『アイドル』なの」** — in context, idolhood is **戦い / battle**.
For Rio, idolhood is not only charm, gratitude, or song. It is a chosen battlefield where desire, dignity, and responsibility are tested.

### Classification

**Core.** Essential for Rio’s performance philosophy, her relationship to Aoi, the manager, BIG4, LizNoir leadership, and the idea that pride can include desperation without becoming weak.

---

## 008 — “プライドの塊” / A Lump of Pride

card\_rio\_008\_st-card-rio-05-hal…

### Summary

After LizNoir transfers to Hoshimi, Rio wants to define the unit’s future direction. Kokoro and Ai have broadened LizNoir’s appeal, and Rio recognizes that the group needs more than singing and dancing if it wants to reach a higher stage. The manager mentions a major Halloween theme-park event, but hesitates because it requires cosplay and character acting. Rio immediately takes offense at the implication that such work is beyond her range and accepts.

On the day, the costume turns out to be a much more exaggerated “zombie nurse” outfit than expected. Rio panics, fears damaging LizNoir’s image, and almost tries to change it. Then Kokoro and Ai message her, looking forward to seeing whether she can do something bold. Rio’s pride flips from embarrassment into resolve. She performs the role as a domineering underworld nurse, turning awkward cosplay into a powerful stage persona. The event becomes a public success and even generates solo-debut speculation, which Rio rejects: her goal is not solo fame, but LizNoir standing at the top.

### What it reveals about Rio

This is one of the funniest Rio stories, but it is also structurally important because it shows her **adaptive pride**. Rio’s initial instinct is image protection: “Will this hurt LizNoir?” That is not shallow vanity; it is leader anxiety. But once she commits, she does not timidly endure the costume. She absorbs the premise into her own authority. The Halloween role succeeds because Rio does not let the costume define her; she makes the costume submit to Rio.

The title **「プライドの塊」** is both joke and truth. Rio is a “lump of pride,” but that pride is productive. It can be provoked into recklessness, but it also gives her the energy to conquer an unfamiliar format.

Kokoro and Ai matter here. Their teasing pressures Rio, but it also gives her a reason to rise. Mature Rio is no longer just the senior judging juniors; she is someone whose juniors can push her into becoming broader.

### Key phrases

**「どんな格好をしようが、私は私よ」** — “No matter what I wear, I am myself.”
The central identity statement of the story.

**「どんな仕事だって、私は、神崎莉央は観客を圧倒してみせる」** — “Whatever the job, I, Kanzaki Rio, will overwhelm the audience.”
This is Rio’s versatility doctrine: unfamiliar work must still become a Rio performance.

**「少なくともLizNoirを続けている間はね」** — her rejection of solo debut while LizNoir continues.
Very important. Rio can shine alone, but she defines her summit through LizNoir.

### Classification

**Important / near-core.** Essential for Rio’s public flexibility, comedic vulnerability, relationship with Kokoro/Ai, and distinction between solo star quality and LizNoir-centered ambition.

---

## 009 — “トップを取るのは私達” / We Are the Ones Who Will Take the Top

card\_rio\_009\_st-card-rio-05-ido…

### Summary

This is a flashback to the period before the NEXT VENUS Grand Prix. Rio meets the young Hoshimi manager while he is still inexperienced and bluntly tests him. She notices his connection to Mana, criticizes him for entering the industry because of a classmate’s invitation, and even drops his business card, implying that a “nice person” cannot survive in the entertainment world. But when he refuses to quit before his idols become full-fledged, Rio recognizes his resolve.

Later, after he watches a LizNoir live, Rio expects him to despair at the gap between LizNoir and his own idols. Instead, he sincerely praises LizNoir’s performance, saying valuable things should be properly evaluated regardless of friend or foe. Rio is caught off guard. She defines LizNoir as performers who will not flatter or curry favor: they will stand at the top through song and dance, blooming nobly. In the final part, she warns him that BanPro is trying to crush Hoshimi, but insists that she and Aoi are not resorting to cheap tricks. She even tests whether he would abandon his idols and transfer to BanPro. When he refuses, she approves.

### What it reveals about Rio

This is a crucial “early Rio” contrast file. It shows Rio before Hoshimi softens her: proud, hostile, guarded, dismissive, and sharp enough to wound. But it also shows the ethical code underneath. She is not simply cruel. She hates irresponsibility, abandonment, and underhandedness. She is severe because she believes the industry punishes weakness and because Saegusa’s departure still reads to her as being abandoned by a formative adult.

The dropped business card is symbolic. Rio is testing whether the manager’s identity is disposable. If he is merely a nice boy attached to Mana’s memory, he will quit. If he stands back up and offers the card again, he may be worth acknowledging. This is why her later trust in him has weight: she first met him by trying to break his polite exterior.

This file also gives one of Rio’s cleanest LizNoir manifestos: **they are performers, not charm merchants**. They will not win by friendliness, fanservice, or “媚び.” They want to bloom nobly through skill.

### Key phrases

**「この業界は単なるいい人じゃ務まらないのよ」** — “This industry cannot be handled by someone who is merely nice.”
A key early Rio judgment: kindness without toughness is unusable.

**「LizNoirはパフォーマーです」** — “LizNoir are performers.”
This is one of the clearest unit identity statements.

**「歌とダンスで頂点に立ちたい」** — “We want to stand at the top through song and dance.”
The older, stricter LizNoir creed.

**「気高く咲き誇りたい」** — “We want to bloom proudly/nobly.”
Excellent black-lily language: elegance, pride, performance, refusal to beg.

### Classification

**Core historical Rio.** This is essential for understanding pre-Hoshimi Rio, her first impression of the manager, her wound around Saegusa, her aversion to underhanded tactics, and LizNoir’s original performance ideology.

---

## 010 — “宣戦布告” / Declaration of War

card\_rio\_010\_st-card-rio-05-kai…

### Summary

Rio begins by discussing Suzu’s Mana cover and declares that LizNoir will not settle into Hoshimi comfort. Hoshimi is pleasant, but she has no intention of becoming “仲良しこよし” — cozy friends. LizNoir will defeat Sunny Peace, Tsuki no Tempest, and TRINITYAiLE, then stand at the top. She asks the manager to bring in different kinds of work so LizNoir can evolve, but initially rejects jobs where she cannot perform.

Then a film-promotion job appears for an action movie starring Kate/Katie Lawrence, a Hollywood star Rio adores. Rio’s professional reserve collapses into fan excitement. She holds a late-night movie screening for the manager, lectures him on Katie, and prepares obsessively for the promotional photoshoot, overtraining, dieting, studying stage combat, and trying to embody the heroine. The manager intervenes when this becomes unhealthy, telling her that she was chosen for her own coolness, not to imitate Katie. Rio accepts this and produces a strong photoshoot that Katie herself praises, leading to a chance meeting. Rio panics, but refuses to miss the opportunity.

### What it reveals about Rio

This file is excellent for Rio’s relationship to admiration. Rio is often framed as the admired one, the rival, the top-class senior. Here, she becomes a fan. That is valuable because it shows she still has an upward gaze. She can admire another woman’s professionalism, body, action discipline, and aura without diminishing herself. But her admiration is dangerous because Rio translates admiration into self-punishing imitation. She does not merely want to promote Katie’s film; she wants to become worthy of the work by almost reconstructing herself.

The manager’s correction is important: Rio does not need to become Katie. She needs to bring **Kanzaki Rio’s coolness** to the project. This repeats one of IDOLY PRIDE’s central identity lessons: inheritance, admiration, and rivalry must not become self-erasure.

The file also expands Rio’s fannish and girlish side. She is intense, knowledgeable, excited, and nervous. Her “cool beauty” image cracks into a very human fan response. Yet her final line — **“チャンスは決して逃がさない！それがLizNoirの神崎莉央よ！”** — restores the Rio grammar: even trembling, she moves.

### Key phrases

**「仲良しこよしをするつもりはないわ」** — “I have no intention of just playing cozy friends.”
Rio’s Hoshimi stance: belonging does not erase rivalry.

**「そのくらいには貴方のことを信用してる」** — “I trust you at least that much.”
A strong marker of her mature manager relationship.

**「ケイティの真似じゃなく、莉央さんらしいクールな格好良さ」** — the manager’s key corrective idea: not imitation, but Rio’s own coolness.

**「チャンスは決して逃がさない」** — “I never let a chance escape.”
A compact Rio motto.

### Classification

**Important.** Core for Rio’s admiration/rivalry psychology, her tendency toward overwork, her relationship with the manager, and the way mature Rio learns not to confuse respect with imitation.

---

## 011 — “思い出を巡れば” / Wandering Through Memories

card\_rio\_011\_st-card-rio-05-kio…

### Summary

Rio is considering singing Mana’s song **「星の海の記憶」** with Kotono. The manager mistakenly thinks she is refusing and tries desperately to persuade her, but Rio has already agreed with Kotono. Amused by his urgency, she asks him to take her through places tied to Mana’s past: meeting spots, early street promotion locations, Mana’s debut stage, and eventually the school where Mana and the manager began. Rio wants to understand the Mana she did not know — and, implicitly, the manager’s Mana.

Throughout the trip, Rio notices how happy the manager looks when talking about Mana. She teases him, but there is also unresolved jealousy and melancholy: Mana remains someone Rio cannot defeat anymore, someone who owns a unique part of the manager’s memory. On the live day, Rio admits she once stepped off the stage after Mana disappeared, unlike Mana, who kept standing until the end. After singing, she and the manager look at the sea of stars. Rio says she felt she met Mana onstage and that everyone may be moving ahead, leaving Mana behind rather than being left behind by her. It is lonely, but probably what Mana would want.

### What it reveals about Rio

This is one of the deepest Rio files. It directly engages Mana, not as abstract rival, but as memory, absence, jealousy, artistic inheritance, and shared grief.

Rio’s relationship to Mana is complex. She admires her, resents her, wants to defeat her, envies her irreplaceable position in the manager’s life, and still wants to sing in a way Mana would accept. The story is especially good because Rio’s jealousy is not petty. It is existential. Mana occupies a place no living rival can occupy: the first idol, the lost miracle, the person who can never be surpassed in ordinary terms because she is no longer in the same time as everyone else.

The line about being “left behind” versus “we are leaving her behind” is enormous. Rio has long felt that Mana escaped, won by dying, or moved beyond reach. Here, she realizes the living are the ones who continue changing. That is both painful and liberating. It means Mana’s memory is not a prison; it is something the living carry forward while necessarily moving beyond her.

This story also shows Rio’s intimacy with the manager at its most emotionally complicated. She wants to know Mana, but also wants to know the manager through Mana. She teases him about “noro-ke” and being too attached, but underneath is the question: if I had been your first idol, would you have been that desperate for me too?

### Key phrases

**「私の知らない彼女と、私の知らない……」** — “The her I didn’t know, and the … I didn’t know…”
She cuts herself off, but the implication is clear: she is also trying to understand the manager’s private past.

**「私が一番じゃないと気が済まない」** — “I’m not satisfied unless I’m first.”
Here it is playful, jealous, and painfully sincere.

**「私は一度、ステージを降りた人間だもの」** — “I am someone who once stepped off the stage.”
Rio’s self-awareness in contrast to Mana.

**「本当は私達が、彼女を置いて行っているのかも」** — “Maybe we are the ones leaving her behind.”
One of Rio’s most mature lines about grief and living after Mana.

**「麻奈が、綺麗だと感じた、この星の海を」** — “This sea of stars that Mana felt was beautiful.”
Memory becomes shared scenery rather than private possession.

### Classification

**Core / foundational.** Essential for Rio’s Mana relationship, grief, jealousy, manager intimacy, living-after-the-dead theme, and mature understanding of inheritance.

---

## 012 — “じっと見ないでちょうだい” / Don’t Stare at Me

card\_rio\_012\_st-card-rio-05-miz…

### Summary

LizNoir accepts a tie-up job with Tokyo Seaside, but neither the manager nor Satomi can accompany them. Rio stays behind to discuss preparation. She and the manager visit the location in advance, and Rio tries on a swimsuit-like stage outfit to check movement, water resistance, and shooting conditions. She becomes flustered when the manager is unsure where to look, then tells him not to stare — despite also needing him to check the outfit.

The second half shifts from playful embarrassment to leadership anxiety. After the first day of shooting, Rio explains that the producer does not fully understand LizNoir, but also admits LizNoir does not fully understand him either, so they must communicate. More importantly, she is unsure how LizNoir should evolve. She recognizes that simply forcing her own view through, as she did in the past, is not enough. The manager tells her that this uncertainty itself is growth: old Rio would not have worried about the gap between her view and others’. Rio accepts that she is still in the middle of becoming stronger. By the end, she has handled the job, learned from it, and acknowledges the manager’s stubbornness as something she wants beside her.

### What it reveals about Rio

This is one of the best “growth-process Rio” files. It begins almost like fanservice comedy, but the real subject is Rio learning to operate without the manager and without total control. The swimsuit embarrassment humanizes her, but the more important part is that she initially frames everything as professional checking: movement, waterproofing, staging, “LizNoirらしさ.” Even when relaxed, Rio professionalizes play.

The leadership content is very strong. Rio’s anxiety is no longer “will I win?” but “what should LizNoir become?” That is a more advanced question. Earlier Rio protected LizNoir by enforcing a defined style: perfection, elegance, coolness, song and dance. Mature Rio knows that Kokoro and Ai changed the group, Hoshimi changed their environment, and broader work may be necessary. But she also fears that openness means losing her axis.

The manager’s line about “我を通す” — imposing one’s own will — is key. Rio used to think her unwavering will was pure strength. Here she is forced to consider that insistence can also be fear: fear of leaving one’s own frame. That insight helps define her mature arc: not abandoning will, but learning when will must bend so that LizNoir can grow.

### Key phrases

**「貴方のかわりにはならない」** — “No one can replace you.”
Rio says this, then immediately deflects. It is a strong emotional admission wrapped in scolding.

**「私が答えを出さないと」** — “I have to find the answer.”
Leader Rio: she feels personally responsible for the unit’s future.

**「我を通すのは強さでもあるけど弱さでもあります」** — “Forcing your will through can be strength, but also weakness.”
The central lesson of the file.

**「成長の途中」** — “in the middle of growth.”
Rio learns to understand uncertainty not as failure, but as transition.

**「頑固なマネージャーさん」** — “stubborn manager.”
Affectionate recognition: she values that he will not back down when idols are at stake.

### Classification

**Core.** Essential for mature Rio as leader, her evolving LizNoir philosophy, her vulnerability, her relationship with the manager, and the transition from rigid pride to adaptive strength.

---

## Batch synthesis: what 007–012 add to Rio

The first Rio batch established that she is no longer alone inside pride: she can be teased, celebrated, supported, fed, comforted, and loved. This second batch explains what she does with that support.

The answer: **she keeps fighting, but with a more complex understanding of what fighting means.**

In 007, fighting means accepting help and throwing away cheap pride for LizNoir’s future. In 008, fighting means refusing to let an embarrassing costume define her. In 009, fighting means insisting on clean rivalry and performer dignity before Hoshimi ever becomes home. In 010, fighting means chasing opportunities without becoming an imitation of the people she admires. In 011, fighting means singing the song of the rival she can no longer defeat and learning that the living must move forward. In 012, fighting means questioning her own rigidity so LizNoir can evolve.

The most important refinement is that Rio’s pride becomes **relational**. She still wants the top, but the top now includes:

-   Aoi, who waited for her and must be answered;

-   Kokoro and Ai, who broaden LizNoir and provoke Rio’s growth;

-   Rui/TRINITYAiLE, rivals who must be fought fairly;

-   Sunny Peace and Tsuki no Tempest, comrades who must still be defeated;

-   Mana, the dead rival who must be honored without being imitated;

-   the manager, whose stubborn care gives Rio permission to be stubborn in a healthier way;

-   fans and staff, whose support turns miracle into earned inevitability.


A compact updated Rio thesis:

**Kanzaki Rio is LizNoir’s battle-center: a proud, elegant, hard-driving performer who began idolhood as family duty and rivalry against miracle, but matures into a leader who understands that true pride is not rigid self-sufficiency. It is the courage to keep fighting beautifully while accepting that other people’s care, memory, challenge, and support are part of the strength that lets her stand.**

**Assistant — Turn 18**

This Rio batch pushes her into some of the most **domestic, romantic-comedic, and origin-revealing** material so far. If the previous batches defined Rio as **battle-center / mature leader / Mana rival / LizNoir pride**, these stories add the parts of her that pride usually hides: daughterhood, embarrassment, jealousy, family approval, technical incompetence in tiny areas, playful trust, and the foundational Aoi bond.

The strongest overall update:

**Rio’s pride is not only stage pride. It is also the fragile pride of a woman who wants to be seen as capable, desirable, irreplaceable, and loved first — but who becomes deeply embarrassed whenever those desires become visible.**

## 013 — “実家に来る？” / “Want to Come to My Family Home?”

card\_rio\_013\_st-card-rio-05-new…

### Summary

After a joint countdown live in Osaka, the manager learns Rio has time off for New Year and suggests she return to Okayama. Rio half-jokingly invites him to visit her family home, partly because he can help explain her transfer to Hoshimi Pro. He accepts sincerely, not realizing the romantic implications of a grown daughter bringing a young man home for New Year.

Rio panics. Her mother immediately understands the situation and teases her by pretending to misunderstand the manager as Rio’s boyfriend. The manager, being earnest and socially dense, keeps praising Rio in ways that sound emotionally loaded: she is dependable, important, “かけがえのない存在” — an irreplaceable presence. Rio becomes increasingly flustered. In the end, her mother reveals she knew all along that he was not actually Rio’s boyfriend and simply enjoyed seeing her daughter’s reaction.

### What it reveals about Rio

This is one of the most important files for Rio as **daughter**. Her mother’s presence collapses the elegant LizNoir center back into a woman who can still be teased at home. Rio’s authority does not work on her mother. The mother sees through her instantly, which is narratively useful because it shows that Rio’s guardedness is learned, not total. There is a Rio beneath the armor whom family can still access.

It also confirms that Rio talks about the manager to her mother with genuine admiration. Her mother says Rio described him as young, talented, promising, and excellent. Rio denies having said “that much,” but the embarrassment implies she has spoken warmly enough that her mother already has a favorable image of him.

The file also strongly reinforces Rio’s **domestic competence**: cooking, household budgeting, café upbringing, and practical life skills. But unlike earlier café-memory stories, this one places those traits in a quasi-marriage frame. The joke is not merely “Rio is embarrassed.” It is that Rio’s private suitability for domestic partnership is real, even if public fan rankings often misread her as too strict or severe.

### Key phrases

**「実家に来る？」** — “Want to come to my family home?”
Rio offers it lightly, then immediately realizes the social meaning after the manager takes it seriously.

**「紹介したい人」** — “someone I want to introduce.”
This phrase is fatal in context. Rio intends “manager,” but the mother hears the romantic template.

**「かけがえのない存在」** — “an irreplaceable presence.”
The manager means “to Hoshimi Pro,” but Rio hears the romantic charge instantly.

**「気が強いんじゃありません。気高いんですよ」** — “You are not strong-willed; you are noble.”
One of the best Rio compliments in the files. It reframes the fan criticism of her “strong personality” into the actual virtue beneath it.

### Classification

**Core for private Rio.** Essential for Rio’s family context, mother relationship, romantic-comedy vulnerability, and the difference between how the public reads her and how people who know her understand her.

---

## 014 — “コスメのCM？” / “A Cosmetics Commercial?”

card\_rio\_014\_st-card-rio-05-paj…

### Summary

After LizNoir becomes BIG4, Rio’s schedule becomes packed. She accepts this as good fortune and jokes that LizNoir will earn enough to get Hoshimi Pro its own building. At a cosmetics event, she receives an offer for a major manicure commercial. At first it seems perfect: glamorous, profitable, good for LizNoir and Hoshimi. Then Rio admits a humiliating secret: she cannot paint her own nails properly.

She usually has makeup staff do it, and in earlier days Aoi secretly helped her. She asks the manager to help her practice because refusing work over a weakness would violate Kanzaki Rio’s pride. The training is awkward and funny: she paints too slowly, incorrectly, and eventually turns the tables by using the manager as a makeup test subject. By the shoot, however, she has mastered the skill through private practice and performs so well that the commercial leads to more work. The final gag is intimate: she says she would only play with the manager this way — **“貴方にしか、しないわよ.”**

### What it reveals about Rio

This file is a perfect Rio microcosm: a tiny weakness becomes a pride crisis, then a training arc, then a professional victory.

The important point is that Rio is not embarrassed because manicures are objectively important. She is embarrassed because she has built her identity around being capable, elegant, and senior. Not being able to do something associated with beauty work feels, to her, like a contradiction of her image. This makes her surprisingly vulnerable: her competence is real, but it is also part of her self-protection.

Aoi’s role is quietly important. He has known this secret for years and protected it. That says a lot about the old Rio/Aoi partnership: beneath their sarcasm and clashes, Aoi has been performing intimate practical care for her since early LizNoir. He can tease her as “お嬢様,” but he also preserves her dignity.

The manager’s role differs from Aoi’s. Rio tells him because she trusts him, and because mature Rio now lets him into the space where her public image is under repair. The later line that she would only mess with him this way is a very Rio kind of intimacy: playful, teasing, slightly domineering, but unmistakably selective.

### Key phrases

**「苦手だから、出来ないから。そんな理由で仕事を断るなんて、この私、神崎莉央のプライドが許さないわ」**
“Refusing work because I’m bad at it or can’t do it — my pride as Kanzaki Rio won’t allow that.”
This is the whole file’s thesis.

**「貴方は秘密を守る人だって、信じてるから」** — “Because I trust you to keep a secret.”
A concise marker of how far the manager has entered Rio’s inner circle.

**「出来るようになるまで特訓する。それ以外の方法を、私は知らないわ」** — “I’ll train until I can do it. I don’t know any other way.”
Pure Rio: no shortcut, no evasion, only practice.

**「貴方にしか、しないわよ」** — “I would only do that to you.”
A playful line, but deeply revealing. Rio’s teasing is selective affection.

### Classification

**Core for everyday Rio.** Essential for her competence anxiety, trust in the manager, Aoi’s long-term care, and the way professional pride turns even a small weakness into a battle to be overcome.

---

## 015 — “葵と私の話” / “The Story of Aoi and Me”

card\_rio\_015\_st-card-rio-05-pas…

### Summary

A documentary about LizNoir’s path to BIG4 brings Rio back to BanPro, her old agency and the birthplace of LizNoir. Saegusa appears, reminding Rio of both gratitude and abandonment: he created LizNoir by bringing Rio and Aoi together, but left BanPro soon after their debut. Rio admits that after Saegusa left, the only people she could truly trust were her mother and Aoi.

The story then shifts into Rio’s account of early LizNoir. Rio and Aoi clashed constantly: Aoi rejected outfits and choreography, skipped meetings, and preferred to dance freely, while Rio was sharper and more controlling than she is now. Before their first solo live, Aoi ran off after an argument. Later, he came to Rio’s apartment, exhausted and near tears, intending to apologize. His stomach growled before he could speak, so Rio bathed him and made pancakes. They talked late into the night about values, performance, the live, and the future of LizNoir. Rio says that may have been the true beginning of their relationship.

Later, she watches old footage and reflects that even embarrassing, immature footage should not be hidden, because the current Rio and current LizNoir exist because of those incomplete beginnings. The story ends with Rio and Aoi recreating their earliest two-person stage, now as BIG4 idols who still return to the starting line in order to keep struggling.

### What it reveals about Rio

This is one of the most important Rio files in the entire set.

It confirms that Rio’s early severity was not just natural arrogance. It was partly distrust. Saegusa’s departure made her feel abandoned by an adult she trusted, and after that, she built walls around herself. This explains why early Rio is so harsh toward the Hoshimi manager in earlier files: she has already learned that adults in the industry can leave.

Aoi’s role becomes foundational. He is not simply Rio’s lazy genius partner. He is the person who remained inside the walls after Saegusa left. The line that Rio could only truly trust her mother and Aoi is enormous. It means Aoi was not optional to her survival as an idol. He was one of the few people through whom she could keep going.

The pancake scene is also a beautiful inversion of Rio’s usual pride. She does not “win” the fight with Aoi by asserting authority. She wins the relationship by feeding him, listening to him, and negotiating artistic difference. Their first real reconciliation is domestic, not theatrical. LizNoir’s elegance begins in a messy apartment, a runaway child, a worried teenage Rio, a bath, pancakes, and a late-night conversation.

The documentary frame gives Rio a mature relationship to her past. She is embarrassed by old footage, but refuses to hide it. That is a major sign of growth: early Rio wanted to appear complete; mature Rio can admit that incompletion is part of the proof of growth.

### Key phrases

**「それ以来、私が心から信じられる存在は、お母さんと……葵だけだった」**
“Since then, the only people I could trust from the bottom of my heart were my mother and… Aoi.”
This is foundational Rio/Aoi evidence.

**「葵と私の話」** — “The story of Aoi and me.”
The title itself frames Aoi as part of Rio’s identity history, not merely unit context.

**「あの日が、私達二人の関係の本当の始まりだったのかもしれない」**
“That day may have been the true beginning of our relationship.”
The pancake reconciliation becomes the emotional origin of LizNoir’s core duo.

**「そういう未熟なところも含めて、今の神崎莉央が――今のLizNoirがある」**
“Including those immature parts, that is how the current Kanzaki Rio — the current LizNoir — exists.”
A mature Rio statement about history, embarrassment, and growth.

**「初心にかえって、もがき続ける覚悟」**
“The resolve to return to our starting point and keep struggling.”
This is mature BIG4 Rio: status does not end struggle.

### Classification

**Foundational / core.** Essential for Rio and Aoi, Saegusa abandonment, BanPro history, trust, early LizNoir, and Rio’s mature relationship to her own imperfection.

---

## 016 — “そんなにじろじろ見ないで” / “Don’t Stare So Much”

card\_rio\_016\_st-card-rio-05-trb…

### Summary

Rio is trying on a modified Christmas event outfit made with alien technology so that Lala and the other visiting aliens can return to their world using the energy generated by audience feelings. The outfit is more revealing than expected because materials had to be stretched for additional costumes. Rio initially presents this as professional necessity, but becomes flustered when the manager compliments her appearance.

After hearing the manager praise Lala as bright, cute, and lovable, Rio becomes jealous, though she does not recognize it immediately. An accident with Lala’s warp machine sends Rio and the manager to another dimension. There, Rio becomes scared and physically clings to him while they search for a way out. She eventually admits he helps her stay calm and that she is glad he is with her. Later, they realize the warp machine may have activated because Rio’s strong emotion — likely jealousy — created enough “thought energy” to trigger it. Rio almost says **やきもち** — jealousy — but stops herself. The story ends with Rio reflecting that being with the person you love is the greatest happiness.

### What it reveals about Rio

This file is ridiculous in plot mechanics, but emotionally very useful.

The alien-warp premise literalizes something Rio normally suppresses: her feelings generate energy. That is the joke, but it is also the character point. Rio’s jealousy is strong enough to move worlds, but she cannot bring herself to name it. She can perform confidence, adultness, stage beauty, and leadership, but when faced with “I wanted to be the one he liked,” her language breaks down.

Lala is an effective contrast. Lala is open, bright, cute, and innocent in a way Rio is not. Rio’s insecurity is not that she wants to become Lala exactly, but that she fears the manager may prefer qualities Rio sees herself as lacking:素直 / straightforwardness, 可愛い / cuteness, easy emotional openness. This connects back to multiple earlier Rio files where she worries she is too severe, too strong-willed, too “not bride-like,” too adult, too intimidating.

The alternate-dimension sequence is also important because it shows Rio allowing physical dependence. She clings to the manager because the situation requires it, but she then chooses to remain close until the warp ends. That is very Rio: she accepts intimacy when it has a practical justification, then quietly extends it because she wants to.

### Key phrases

**「そんなにじろじろ見ないで」** — “Don’t stare so much.”
The title captures Rio’s contradiction: she wants to be admired, but direct admiration overwhelms her.

**「貴方が一緒だから、私は落ち着いていられる」**
“Because you are with me, I can stay calm.”
A rare plain admission of reliance.

**「これは私の本音よ」** — “This is my true feeling.”
Rio explicitly marks the admission as sincere.

**「強い想いがエネルギーになる」** — “Strong feelings become energy.”
The sci-fi joke is also the emotional thesis of the file.

**「もしかして、あの時私は……やきも……」**
“Could it be that back then I was… jeal…”
She cannot finish “jealous.” That incompletion is the character.

**「好きな人と一緒にいられるのが、一番の幸せよね」**
“Being together with the person you love is the greatest happiness.”
A startlingly direct Rio line, displaced onto Lala but obviously resonant.

### Classification

**Important / emotionally core.** The plot is comedic crossover chaos, but the emotional content is valuable for Rio’s jealousy, vulnerability, desire to be seen as cute, and dependence on the manager.

---

## 017 — “私のプライドはズタズタよ” / “My Pride Is in Tatters”

card\_rio\_017\_st-card-rio-05-wed…

### Summary

Rio is offered a bridal commercial, but hesitates because she does not see herself as a conventional bride image. The manager pushes harder than usual because he already knows Rio has ranked poorly in an “idol you’d want as a bride” poll. Rio later sees the ranking, where Haruko ranks first while Rio barely makes the list and is last within LizNoir. The fan comments sting because they praise her while undercutting her: she seems strong-willed, strict, useful for living properly, but not necessarily warm. Kokoro teases her mercilessly, Aoi jokes she should be the groom instead, and Rio’s pride collapses.

She accepts the bridal job as revenge against the image. In rehearsal, she tries to create “the strongest bride,” first overperforming cuteness, then gradually articulating a Rio-like marriage ideal: communication, mutual stimulation, supporting each other, flexible housework, and wanting to be loved most. At the shoot, she becomes deeply embarrassed because the manager is unexpectedly present instead of Satomi. In the end, her dignified bridal image succeeds. The public now praises her as an independent, cool, strong bride. Rio enjoys the reversal but says LizNoir taking the top comes before any real marriage.

### What it reveals about Rio

This file is a direct companion to both the romance-game file and the family-home file. Together, they map Rio’s relation to romance, marriage, and public desirability.

The key is that Rio is wounded by the ranking not because she secretly wants to become a conventional domestic ideal, but because she cannot tolerate being told there is a field where she loses — especially to her own members. Yet the comments sting because they are not pure insults. They identify real traits: strictness, intensity, discipline, self-control, high standards. Rio’s problem is that these virtues are being read as romantic deficits.

The bridal job lets her solve this not by pretending to be Haruko or Sakura, but by constructing a **Rio-like bride**: cool, dignified, self-reliant, communicative, loyal, professionally driven, and intensely wanting to be loved first. The line **「私を一番好きでいてほしい」** is one of the most emotionally naked Rio lines in the corpus. Her ideal marriage is not submission, sweetness, or decorative softness. It is mutual respect plus absolute emotional priority.

The shoot embarrassment also matters. Rio can wear revealing costumes, swimsuits, cosplay, and stagewear professionally, but the wedding dress embarrasses her more because it carries personal meaning. She says it is more embarrassing than a swimsuit. That tells us the issue is not exposure; it is emotional implication. A wedding dress makes her feel seen not as performer-body, but as woman, partner, and possible future wife.

### Key phrases

**「私のプライドはズタズタよ！」** — “My pride is in tatters!”
Classic Rio comedy, but also the emotional engine: she converts humiliation into work.

**「神崎莉央が、花嫁でも一番だってことを世間に認めさせてあげる」**
“I’ll make the world acknowledge that Kanzaki Rio is number one even as a bride.”
Romance becomes competition, because that is how Rio metabolizes vulnerability.

**「私を一番好きでいてほしい」**
“I want them to love me the most.”
This is probably the most direct articulation of Rio’s romantic desire.

**「私が選んだパートナーは、私だけを一生愛してくれるの！」**
“The partner I choose will love only me for their whole life!”
Jealous, possessive, idealistic, and very Rio.

**「貴方に相談もなく私が結婚する訳ないでしょ」**
“There’s no way I’d get married without consulting you.”
A joking line, but it places the manager as indispensable to any major future choice.

### Classification

**Core for romantic/private Rio.** Essential for her public image anxiety, desire to be loved first, bridal/marriage ideal, manager intimacy, and the recurring pattern where humiliation becomes professional conquest.

---

# Batch synthesis: what 013–017 add to Rio

This batch makes Rio more complete by showing that her battle-center identity has a private emotional underside.

Across these five files, Rio repeatedly encounters situations where her usual strengths become unstable:

She is competent, but cannot paint her nails.
She is proud, but her mother can tease her into helpless embarrassment.
She is elegant, but wedding work makes her feel more exposed than a swimsuit.
She is a leader, but old footage shows her immaturity.
She is cool, but jealousy toward Lala almost activates alien technology.
She is a top idol, but bride rankings and fan comments can still wound her.
She is independent, but Aoi and the manager know secrets she cannot tell everyone.

The major refinement is that Rio’s pride now has three layers.

**Public pride:** LizNoir must be excellent, dignified, and victorious.
**Professional pride:** Rio must accept jobs, overcome weaknesses, and make every format into a Rio performance.
**Private pride:** Rio wants to be chosen, loved first, understood correctly, and not reduced to “strict,” “scary,” or “not cute.”

That private layer is the new material here. Rio does not merely want audiences to admire her. She wants the people close to her to see that her severity contains tenderness, loyalty, domesticity, longing, and vulnerability.

The strongest updated formulation:

**Kanzaki Rio is a proud adult idol whose elegance is built over daughterhood, loss, distrust, rivalry, labor, and longing. Her mature charm lies in the gap between how completely she can command a stage and how easily she becomes flustered when the question is not “Can you win?” but “Can you let yourself be loved?”**

The major update:

**Rio’s mature character is not only pride plus vulnerability.
