# 012 — “莉央のお世話で大変だ” / “Taking Care of Rio Is Hard”

card\_aoi\_012\_st-card-aoi-05-pas…

## Summary

This card is framed around a LizNoir documentary shoot tracing the group’s path to BIG4. Aoi wanders away during a delay and goes to an old park near BanPro where she used to dance after debut. The manager finds her, and Aoi explains that Rio learned about this place after one of their early fights and would come drag her back whenever she disappeared.

Saegusa appears, and the story shifts into early LizNoir history. Aoi jokes that Saegusa put her and Rio together and then abandoned them, leaving her to deal with Rio. Saegusa recalls scouting Aoi when she was a child dancing in a park — and Aoi initially assuming he was a suspicious adult.

Aoi then recounts the first major Rio/Aoi fight. Early Rio was intense, strict, and constantly angry about Aoi’s fanservice, attitude, and costume complaints. Aoi calls her almost a **狂犬** — a mad dog. The argument centered on idol costuming and how much “frilly” idol presentation Aoi should accept. After Aoi ran off, Saegusa told her Rio had been desperately searching for her. Aoi went to Rio’s apartment and found her crying while stress-eating a tower of pancakes. Rio made Aoi shower, then cooked pancakes for her too, and they talked through LizNoir’s direction, the one-man live, and their future. Aoi stayed overnight.

The final section adds another private Rio/Aoi memory. Aoi watches old MV footage and remembers how close the staging was; Rio had been embarrassed by the physical proximity. Aoi also recalls Rio once trying to communicate by silently squishing Aoi’s cheeks after their fight, worried Aoi might run away again. Aoi says Rio’s cute parts are something only she needs to know. Then she reveals she and Rio are going to a hotel together to eat, stay overnight, and reminisce without Kokoro and Ai interrupting.

## What it reveals about Aoi

This is foundational Aoi/Rio material.

From Aoi’s perspective, early Rio is not just “strict leader.” Rio is someone Aoi found fascinating because, beneath her tension, her dancing was powerful and passionate. Aoi says that when they first danced together, she naturally thought: **if I’m going to form a unit, this is the person.** That matters because Aoi’s bond with Rio is bodily before it is verbal. She chooses Rio because Rio’s movement speaks to her.

The story also reverses Rio’s caretaker image. Rio often appears as the one managing Aoi, but Aoi frames herself as the one who had to handle Rio’s intensity. The title itself — **“Taking Care of Rio Is Hard”** — is affectionate exaggeration, but it captures their real mutuality. Rio bathes, feeds, and manages Aoi; Aoi loosens, teases, reads, and disarms Rio.

The pancake scene is especially important because it is the origin of their domestic intimacy. Aoi discovers Rio’s softness not through confession, but through a ridiculous private image: severe Rio crying while eating pancakes. Aoi’s affection often begins with amusement, but it is not shallow. She remembers because it revealed the hidden person beneath Rio’s armor.

The “only I need to know Rio’s cute parts” line is also massive. It makes Aoi protective of private Rio. She does not just enjoy teasing Rio publicly; she also withholds certain Rio memories as shared property. That is exactly the “closed-world partner” dynamic that makes Rio/Aoi feel so yuri-coded: not necessarily overt dating language, but private access, bodily familiarity, teasing intimacy, sleepover/hotel domesticity, and old wounds only the two of them fully share.

## Key phrases

**「組むならこの人がいいって、自然と思った」**
“I naturally thought, if I’m going to pair with someone, this is the person.”
Aoi’s original selection of Rio as partner.

**「僕は、莉央と二人で踊っていられれば良かった」**
“I would have been fine just dancing with Rio.”
One of the clearest statements of early Aoi’s closed two-person LizNoir worldview.

**「狂犬莉央と猛獣使い葵」**
“Mad-dog Rio and beast-tamer Aoi.”
Aoi’s biased but revealing comic myth of their origin.

**「莉央の可愛いとこは、僕だけ知ってればいいんだ」**
“Rio’s cute parts are something only I need to know.”
Probably one of the strongest Aoi-side Rio/Aoi intimacy lines so far.

## Classification

**Foundational / core.** Essential for Aoi/Rio, early LizNoir, Saegusa, Aoi’s memory of Rio, and the domestic-private layer underneath LizNoir’s black-lily image.

---

# 013 — “二月のチョコだけが違う” / “Only February Chocolate Is Different”

card\_aoi\_013\_st-card-aoi-05-vln…

## Summary

Before Valentine’s Day, Aoi is practicing late for a Valentine event because Kokoro is enthusiastic about it. The manager walks her home. Aoi eats okara cookies, complains that Rio gets angry when she eats during lessons, and then notices all the chocolate advertisements around town.

This leads to a very Aoi question: why is Valentine’s chocolate special? Chocolate tastes good any time, so why does February chocolate carry extra meaning? The manager explains that the event gives people a reason to confess feelings they might not otherwise be brave enough to express.

Aoi says she receives lots of chocolate from Rio, fans, and school friends, but she is not only a recipient. She reveals that she makes handmade chocolates every year for Rio and her family. This surprises the manager, so Aoi decides to make chocolates for everyone at Hoshimi Pro this year.

She researches everyone’s preferences, including not just LizNoir but Sunny Peace, Tsuki no Tempest, TRINITYAiLE, and others. She plans individualized decorations and flavors. The manager tells her that she does not need perfect information; what matters is conveying feeling. Aoi takes this seriously.

On Valentine’s Day, she gives personalized chocolates to everyone. Then, privately, she gives the manager his chocolate. Because he earlier warned her not to give presents to men in public, she has been carrying it around waiting for the right moment. She is embarrassed because of what he said about Valentine’s being confession-coded, but she insists on giving it that day because otherwise the feeling will not properly communicate.

## What it reveals about Aoi

This card is extremely useful for Aoi’s relationship to emotional expression.

Aoi is usually direct, but Valentine’s Day introduces a kind of symbolic emotional grammar that even she finds strange. Her initial question — why is February chocolate different? — is very Aoi. She does not naturally accept social meaning just because everyone else does. She wants to know why an ordinary object becomes emotionally charged.

But once she understands the mechanism, she uses it sincerely. She does not dismiss Valentine’s. She decides that if chocolate is a medium for feeling, then she should use it properly.

The fact that Aoi has always made chocolate for Rio and family is important. It shows that Aoi’s care is not as careless as she looks. She has a yearly ritual of handmade affection, and Rio is included in the innermost category alongside family. That strengthens the Rio/Aoi domestic bond without needing overt romance.

The manager chocolate is a different kind of intimacy. Aoi becomes embarrassed not because she has suddenly transformed into a standard romantic heroine, but because the manager has taught her the social meaning of the act. Once the act has meaning, she refuses to empty it out. She says it must be given today, because today is what lets the feeling be transmitted.

## Key phrases

**「この二月のチョコだけが違うって訳じゃないと思うんだけどな」**
“I don’t think only February chocolate is somehow different.”
Aoi questioning symbolic social rituals.

**「これでも毎年、莉央と家族には手作りしてるんだよ」**
“I make handmade ones every year for Rio and my family.”
Very important: Rio is placed in Aoi’s closest gift circle.

**「大切なのは、心を込めてプレゼントすること」**
“The important thing is putting your heart into the present.”
The lesson Aoi internalizes.

**「今日渡さないと気持ちが伝わらないでしょ」**
“If I don’t give it today, the feeling won’t get across.”
Aoi learns the event’s emotional grammar and uses it deliberately.

**「責任取って、早く受け取ってほしいな」**
“Take responsibility and hurry up and accept it.”
Aoi’s embarrassment becomes teasing demand.

## Classification

**Core for emotional-expression Aoi.** Essential for her gift-giving, Rio/family intimacy, manager romantic-comedy tension, and her way of turning confusing social symbolism into sincere action.

---

# 014 — “彼女が怪我を？” / “She Got Injured?”

card\_aoi\_014\_st-card-aoi-05-wdn…

## Summary

After an underground idol-tournament event, Aoi continues training for a solo battle at the same underground venue. She has accepted a challenge from an idol she met there. The manager is alarmed because she has once again decided things without properly reporting them.

The opponent turns out to be **DJばぁば**, an elderly idol and member of BEIJU, whom Aoi respects. But DJばぁば has injured her back from overtraining. The manager wants to cancel the battle. DJばぁば refuses because fans are waiting and because her dream is to remain an idol for life.

Aoi sympathizes deeply. She understands the desire to answer fans regardless of age or status. But she also sees that DJばぁば cannot safely battle. The compromise is a duet: Aoi supports DJばぁば physically onstage while hiding the support from the audience’s sightline, and she creates an improvised choreography that lets DJばぁば perform without worsening her injury.

Afterward, Aoi says the dance was not born from her alone. It was created by DJばぁば’s desire to make fans smile and remain a lifelong idol. Aoi resolves to become an idol and adult who can think that strongly about fans.

In the final part, Aoi reveals that meeting DJばぁば helped her overcome anxiety about idol temporality. Her parents had warned her that idol work cannot last forever, but DJばぁば proves that if one loves being an idol, one can continue as long as one refuses to give up. Aoi concludes that there is no limit to continuing what one loves.

## What it reveals about Aoi

This is one of Aoi’s most important adulthood cards.

The earlier Aoi worldview is: **I dance because I like dancing.**
This card expands that into: **I can keep living as an idol because loving the stage has no age limit.**

DJばぁば is a mirror from the future. She shows Aoi an answer to the question: what happens to an idol after youth, novelty, and conventional career timing pass? For Rio, idol temporality often creates anxiety about the top, future, and retirement. For Aoi, DJばぁば gives a liberating answer: there may be no fixed end if the performer keeps finding ways to stand.

The stage support is also a major Aoi skill demonstration. Aoi is usually framed as spontaneous and careless, but here she is extraordinarily precise: she hides physical support within performance blocking, protects the audience’s illusion, reduces bodily strain, and improvises choreography around another person’s limitation. That is mature Aoi as choreographer-caretaker.

It also clarifies Aoi’s ethics. She respects DJばぁば’s desire to perform, but she does not simply romanticize self-destruction. She finds a form that honors both dream and body. That is very mature: not “perform no matter what,” but “make performance possible without betraying the performer.”

## Key phrases

**「野良アイドルもプロも関係ない」**
“Street idol or professional, it doesn’t matter.”
Aoi respects the same stage-feeling across status lines.

**「僕がばぁばをステージで支える」**
“I’ll support Baaba onstage.”
Aoi turning competition into care.

**「ばぁばがいたから、このダンスが生まれたんだ」**
“This dance was born because Baaba was there.”
Aoi’s mature choreography philosophy: movement emerges from people, not ego.

**「好きなことを続けるのに、限界なんてない」**
“There is no limit to continuing what you love.”
The card’s thesis.

**「僕はいつまでもアイドルでいられるんだ。僕が諦めない限り、ずっと」**
“I can be an idol forever. As long as I don’t give up.”
Aoi’s future horizon.

## Classification

**Core for adult/future Aoi.** Essential for Aoi’s idol lifespan philosophy, fan-care, choreography under constraint, and her movement from self-expression to lifelong vocation.

---

# 015 — “ドラマの中のことなのに” / “Even Though It’s Only in a Drama”

card\_aoi\_015\_st-card-aoi-05-wed…

## Summary

Aoi receives an acting offer for a mystery drama. At first she is prepared for action or even a corpse role, but learns the role is a romance: a couple opposed by those around them, eventually united in a wedding scene.

Aoi freezes. She has no dating experience, no clear image of romance, and no desire to wear a wedding dress. She consults LizNoir. Rio starts crying at the thought of Aoi “going off as a bride,” even though it is only a drama. Ai thinks Aoi would look good in a wedding dress. Kokoro teases her by implying she could take the job and look better.

The manager persuades Aoi that acting is another form of expression, like dance. Aoi accepts, but asks the manager to practice with her because he is the only nearby man. During rehearsal, she struggles with the bride role but finds the groom’s lines easier. She can naturally say: **“I’ll never let you go; I’ll make you happy for life.”** The manager gets flustered, and Aoi observes that his reaction is cute.

Later, after practice, Aoi cooks for the manager because he has not eaten. Watching him enjoy her food gives her the first concrete emotional clue: maybe marriage is something like being happy that someone is happy because of what you made for them. But she rejects the script’s passive idea that someone else will “make her happy.” She thinks marriage sounds constraining, and she does not like the line “I’ll make you happy” when said to her.

The manager suggests bringing Aoi’s own view into the role. The script is adjusted. In the final wedding scene, when the groom says he will make her happy, Aoi-as-heroine answers: **No — I will make you happy.** The result is praised as a cool, Aoi-like bride. Aoi then jokingly tells the manager he could come as her bride, because she will definitely make him happy.

## What it reveals about Aoi

This is the most important romance/gender-role Aoi card so far.

Aoi does not fit conventional bride fantasy. She is not hostile to affection, care, or partnership, but she strongly resists a romance script where the woman is the passive recipient of happiness. The wedding dress feels like armor to her: heavy, restrictive, and battle-like. That metaphor is perfectly Aoi. She does not read the dress as pure softness; she reads it as something to move and fight inside.

Her natural access point is the groom role. She understands wanting to protect, lead, and make someone happy. This does not mean she is “masculine” in a simplistic way, but it does show that Aoi’s romantic imagination is active rather than receptive. If she loves, she wants to act. If she marries, she wants to be the one who brings happiness, not someone placed in another person’s care.

Rio’s reaction is also important. Rio cries at the thought of Aoi as a bride, which reinforces how emotionally possessive and tender Rio is around Aoi. The comedy works because Rio knows it is fiction and still reacts as though Aoi is being taken away.

The manager side is also strongly romantic-comedy coded. Aoi chooses him for male practice, cooks for him, observes his cute reactions, then proposes the reversed “you come as my bride” joke. But even that line is less standard flirtation than Aoi turning marriage into Aoi-form: role reversal, playful dominance, practical care, and a promise that she will make the other person happy.

## Key phrases

**「恋愛とか結婚のイメージなんてわかない」**
“I can’t picture romance or marriage.”
Aoi’s starting point.

**「花婿の台詞なら、ちょっと出来る気がする」**
“I feel like I could do the groom’s lines a little.”
The central role-reversal clue.

**「僕のご飯で、誰かが幸せそうにしてるのはいいものだね」**
“It feels nice when someone looks happy because of my food.”
Aoi discovering domestic care as emotional expression.

**「君が僕を幸せにするんじゃなくて、僕が、君を幸せにするんだ」**
“You are not going to make me happy. I am going to make you happy.”
Aoi’s romance thesis.

**「いっそ{user}君が嫁に来るっていうのはどう？」**
“How about you come as my bride?”
Aoi’s playful reversal of marriage convention.

## Classification

**Core for romantic/private Aoi.** Essential for Aoi’s relationship to gender roles, marriage, acting, manager intimacy, and Rio’s attachment to Aoi.

---

# 016 — “歌とダンスで生きていく” / “I Will Live Through Song and Dance”

card\_aoi\_016\_st-card-aoi-05-yuk…

## Summary

After LizNoir becomes BIG4, Aoi is busy but in excellent condition. She says she has prepared for the workload and even warns Kokoro and Ai to manage their conditioning. She also says she prefers work over school and has little interest in school life. She plays sports with boys, gets chocolate from girls, and seems socially fine, but she wants more work.

The next day, the manager learns from Aoi’s parents that her school attendance is low enough that she may repeat the year. Aoi has not done her homework or special assignments and treats it almost like someone else’s problem because it does not affect work. Her proposed solution is to quit school:

**「僕は歌とダンスで生きていく」**
“I will live through song and dance.”

The manager and Rio strongly oppose this. They argue that school experience is still a unique life experience and that Aoi should not prematurely narrow her world. Aoi does not really understand, but because both Rio and the manager insist, she accepts. The manager restricts her work until she finishes homework and assignments. She tries to get him to do some of it, jokes, complains, and reveals small body-image insecurities, but ultimately completes three weeks of work.

When she returns, she admits that the assignments became interesting once she started and that this too may be an experience only available while she is a student. She still says dancing is like breathing and that she secretly kept moving her body even during the restriction.

The manager then arranges a summer-themed shoot for LizNoir so Aoi can have at least a little summer experience despite losing most of her break to work and homework. Aoi immediately causes fireworks chaos by launching a parachute firework during Rio’s shoot, then happily spends time with the members. At the end, she invites the manager into the group’s summer circle, saying there is no need for distance between him and LizNoir now.

## What it reveals about Aoi

This card is core because it states Aoi’s life-principle directly.

Aoi does not treat school as hostile. She simply does not see the point of anything that is not song, dance, work, or embodied experience. That is both beautiful and dangerous. Her conviction is sincere, but it can narrow her world. Rio and the manager function as protective brakes: they do not deny her talent, but they refuse to let talent become an excuse for skipping life.

The line **“踊ることは呼吸と一緒”** — dancing is like breathing — is probably one of the most important Aoi lines in the corpus. It explains why she wanders off to dance, why she cannot stay still, why she processes acting through expression, why she bonds through movement, and why her future anxiety is about whether she can keep performing forever.

The card also shows that Aoi’s ordinary social life is not absent. She is not isolated at school; she plays sports with boys and receives Valentine chocolate from girls. But ordinary adolescence is not her center. She must be taught that “ordinary” is not worthless just because it is not stage work.

The ending is also useful for Aoi’s relationship to the manager and LizNoir. She calls him like another older brother, but also folds him into the group’s summer, asks about his love life, and says a work-filled summer is “like us.” This supports the idea that Aoi’s intimacy with him has several layers: older-brother comfort, manager trust, playful teasing, and occasional romantic-comedy charge.

## Key phrases

**「僕は歌とダンスで生きていく」**
“I will live through song and dance.”
Aoi’s clearest vocational declaration.

**「踊ることは呼吸と一緒なんだ」**
“Dancing is the same as breathing.”
Probably the most compact Aoi thesis line.

**「君と莉央がそう言うなら、そうなのかもね」**
“If you and Rio say so, maybe that’s how it is.”
Shows who can make Aoi accept a limit: Rio and the manager.

**「兄貴がもう一人増えた気分だよ」**
“It feels like I’ve gained another big brother.”
Aoi’s protective-manager framing.

**「今更、僕達と君にそんな遠慮はいらないよ」**
“There’s no need for that kind of distance between you and us now.”
Aoi bringing the manager into LizNoir’s circle.

## Classification

**Foundational / core.** Essential for Aoi’s vocation, school/work conflict, Rio-manager protective axis, dancing-as-breathing, and Aoi’s need to broaden experience without losing her core.

---

# Batch synthesis: what 012–016 add to Aoi

This batch deepens Aoi in five major ways.

## 1\. Aoi’s origin with Rio is more intimate than “partner” alone suggests

The past card confirms that Rio/Aoi began with conflict, bodily recognition, domestic reconciliation, and private access. Aoi saw Rio’s dance before she understood Rio’s burden. Rio fed and housed Aoi before they fully knew how to communicate. Aoi now holds onto Rio’s embarrassing and cute memories as private treasure.

This makes Aoi central to Rio’s identity in a way that is not replaceable by the manager or by later LizNoir members. Aoi knew Rio when Rio was still forming her armor.

## 2\. Aoi’s care is handmade, bodily, and practical

Chocolate, cooking, choreography, supporting DJばぁば, dragging the manager into activities, bringing him into group time — Aoi’s care rarely sounds conventionally soft. It is enacted.

She makes food.
She makes dances.
She supports bodies.
She invites people to move.
She gives gifts with precise symbolic timing.
She notices when an experience could become performance.

Aoi’s love language is not “I will explain my feelings.” It is **I will make something with my body and give it to you.**

## 3\. Aoi resists passive femininity, but not intimacy

The wedding card is crucial. Aoi does not reject closeness, care, marriage, or romance because she is cold. She rejects scripts where she is made passive. She can understand feeding someone, making them happy, protecting them, leading them, and asking them to stay. She struggles with being the one who is “made happy.”

That means Aoi’s romantic coding often appears as role reversal: she leads the dance, says the groom’s lines, makes the food, offers to make the manager happy, and treats the wedding dress like armor.

## 4\. Aoi’s future expands from “dance because I like it” to “I can remain an idol as long as I keep choosing it”

The DJばぁば card is a major future-orientation piece. It answers the adult anxiety that surrounds idol stories: what happens when youth passes? For Aoi, the answer becomes liberating rather than tragic. If she loves song and dance, she can keep finding forms for them.

This does not erase practical limits. The story is careful to show injury and bodily care. But it rejects the idea that idolhood must end because of age alone.

## 5\. Aoi needs Rio and the manager as brakes, but for different reasons

Rio and the manager are the two people who can reliably make Aoi stop and reconsider.

**Rio** can stop Aoi because Rio is Aoi’s original partner, the person whose dancing, care, anger, and vulnerability shaped Aoi’s early idol life.

**The manager** can stop Aoi because he offers practical protection and emotional accompaniment. He is a meddlesome older-brother figure, but also someone Aoi trusts enough to accept advice from and tease romantically when the situation allows.

Together, they prevent Aoi’s freedom from becoming self-neglect.

---

# What this batch adds to Rio’s eventual synthesis

For Rio, this batch is invaluable.

It shows that Aoi does not simply “belong” to Rio as an old partner. Aoi actively remembers, protects, and curates the private Rio whom others do not see. She remembers crying-pancake Rio, cheek-squishing Rio, embarrassed-close-MV Rio, and hotel-pancake Rio. Aoi’s memory is one of the main archives of Rio’s hidden softness.

It also clarifies why Rio worries about Aoi so intensely. Aoi can quit school because dance matters more, run off to old parks, enter underground tournaments, drag people into dangerous activities, and treat public image as secondary to what feels alive. Rio’s strictness is partly exasperation, but partly the fear of someone who knows exactly how far Aoi will go if no one pulls her back.

At the same time, Rio needs Aoi because Aoi protects the part of Rio that still wants performance to be fun, bodily, intimate, and alive. Rio gives LizNoir weight; Aoi gives it breath.

---

# Refined Aoi thesis after this batch

**Igawa Aoi is LizNoir’s free body and living breath: a self-possessed dancer who intends to live through song and dance, but whose growth lies in discovering that life cannot be only technique or motion. It must also include school, gifts, food, age, injury, fans, ordinary summers, strange romance scripts, handmade care, and the private bonds that let her remain herself.**

And for Rio/Aoi specifically:

**Aoi is the keeper of Rio’s hidden Rio: the person who remembers the crying girl behind the black lily, the partner who can tease Rio’s dignity without betraying it, and the one whose own freedom keeps Rio’s pride from becoming too heavy to dance.**

The strongest update:

**Aoi is not socially empty or emotionally indifferent. She is selective. She often seems careless because she does not perform social emotion in the expected way, but the message/bond layer shows that she notices people constantly — their fatigue, preferences, talent, reactions, fear, growth, food tastes, praise, and vulnerabilities. She just converts most of that attention into teasing, movement, food, or deadpan requests.**

# Bond stories — Aoi as senior, oddball, and quiet caretaker

bond\_aoi\_001\_aoi

## 001 — “マッスルなんでしょ？” / “You’re Muscle, Right?”

Aoi asks Ai to watch a choreography idea during lunch, then immediately pulls her into trying a difficult double turn. Ai panics, but Aoi insists she can do it because Ai’s physical ability may even surpass Aoi’s. When Ai succeeds, Aoi says they should repeat it ten more times because “ten is light,” and Ai protests that Aoi’s “light” is extremely heavy for her.

This is a good miniature of Aoi as senior. She is encouraging, but her standards are absurdly embodied. She genuinely believes Ai can do the harder thing, and that belief is not empty praise. The problem is that Aoi’s baseline for “reasonable training” is calibrated to Aoi, not to normal people.

**Contribution:** important for Aoi’s senior mode. She sees Ai’s potential clearly, but teaching for her means pulling someone bodily into the experience and assuming they will catch up.

---

## 002 — “シベリアトラを作るから” / “I’ll Make a Siberian Tiger”

Aoi and Ai bake cookies. Aoi suggests making animal-shaped cookies that fit each recipient. Kokoro becomes a “little devil,” while Rio is too strong to be merely a lion: Aoi decides Rio is more like a Siberian tiger — maybe a man-eating Siberian tiger.

This is small comedy, but it is very good for Aoi’s perception of Rio. Rio is not “mother,” “older sister,” or simply “leader” in Aoi’s imagination. Rio is a powerful, dangerous, beautiful animal. That keeps the Rio/Aoi dynamic from collapsing into domestic softness. Aoi can receive Rio’s care, but she still sees Rio as something wild and formidable.

**Contribution:** useful flavor for Aoi’s handmade care and her affectionate, teasing mythologization of Rio.

---

## 003 — “絶対に愛を怒らせない” / “I’ll Never Make Ai Angry”

Aoi and Ai play a dance game. Aoi breezes through it and gives Ai technical advice about reducing wasted motion. Ai struggles, then chooses a punching machine as her preferred game and hits it so hard that the machine seems damaged. Aoi solemnly vows never to make Ai angry.

This bond story shows a nice exchange of strengths. Aoi is the natural dancer; Ai is the physical powerhouse. Aoi can dominate rhythm/dance games, but Ai’s raw power shocks even her.

**Contribution:** important for Aoi/Ai. Aoi respects ability wherever it appears, and Ai’s “muscle” stops being just a joke; it is a real capability Aoi must account for.

---

## 004 — “このピンクのは……” / “This Pink One Is…”

Aoi and Ai are cleaning. Ai accidentally uses Rio’s current toothbrush to scrub narrow gaps. Ai panics and says she will resign from LizNoir. Aoi calmly prevents disaster by suggesting they go buy replacement consumables for everyone and frame it as restocking during cleaning.

This is one of the better “Aoi is more practical than she looks” scenes. She does not panic. She identifies the social problem — Rio will be angry, Ai will overreact — and solves it with a plausible domestic workaround.

**Contribution:** important for Aoi as quiet fixer. She is not merely chaos; she can manage household disaster with surprising calm.

---

## 005 — “頼もしいな、千紗は” / “How Reliable You Are, Chisa”

Aoi accompanies Chisa to a horror-film location. Aoi says she will protect Chisa because the area is dark, but when a dog appears, Aoi hides behind Chisa. Chisa then promises to protect Aoi.

This is a clean vulnerability beat. Aoi’s fear of dogs is not mild dislike; it overrides her coolness instantly. The scene also lets Chisa become the protective one, which is rare because Aoi often occupies the physically capable side of pair dynamics.

**Contribution:** useful for Aoi’s dog fear and for showing that her courage is domain-specific. She can face stages, fights, physical risk, and strangers, but dogs short-circuit her.

---

## 006 — “こんにゃくを怪人にしてやる” / “I’ll Turn Konjac into a Monster”

Aoi falls asleep and recounts a dream where another version of herself turns konjac into a monster, while Chisa appears as a magical Sunny Peace hero and defeats it with “Full-Power Absolute Shining Flash.”

This story is mostly absurd, but it gives texture to Aoi’s imagination. Her dreams remix food dislikes, Rio’s errands, Hoshimi members, and tokusatsu/magical-hero logic into deadpan surrealism. It also shows how exhausted she can be: she is the kind of person who may be physically superhuman in dance but still falls asleep anywhere.

**Contribution:** flavor. Good for Aoi’s weird imagination, fatigue, and receptivity to other units’ aesthetics.

---

## 007 — “そのための特訓” / “Training for That”

On an off day, Aoi practices breakfalls, sliding, and wall jumps for increasingly absurd hypothetical emergencies: being thrown by an attacker, sliding into a store before closing, or saving Rio from a crow. Sakura eventually realizes Aoi is probably just bored and invites her out.

This is a perfect Aoi off-day story. She cannot simply “do nothing.” Boredom becomes training, and training becomes a ridiculous justification engine. The crow line also matters because even her absurd preparedness includes Rio. Protecting Rio has been folded into her physical imagination.

**Contribution:** strong flavor. Aoi’s leisure instinct is movement; even nonsense training is how she keeps herself alive.

---

## 008 — “アイスの蓋は舐めない” / “I Don’t Lick Ice Cream Lids”

Aoi goes out at night to dance because an off day has made her body feel dull. Sakura praises LizNoir’s cool, stylish dance style, and Aoi summarizes LizNoir’s creed through deadpan nonsense: **“LizNoir does not need smiles; you do not put maple syrup on pizza.”** Later she says **“LizNoir does not need smiles; you do not lick ice cream lids.”**

This is funny, but it is also revealing. Aoi understands LizNoir’s style as a set of aesthetic taboos: not because she thinks smiles are evil, but because style depends on what you do not do. She expresses that not through Rio’s refined language, but through food analogies and absurd maxims.

**Contribution:** important for Aoi’s LizNoir style voice. She can articulate the unit’s aesthetic, but in a wonderfully Aoi-shaped way: cool philosophy filtered through snacks.

# Message layer — Aoi’s actual text voice

message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m…

The message bundles are extremely valuable for voice. Aoi’s texting style is short, blunt, casual, and rhythmically strange. She often opens with “やあ,” “ねえ,” or a stamp, then asks something abrupt, changes topics midstream, and treats absurd requests as if they were reasonable.

Her message grammar is often:

**casual greeting → strange question/request → deadpan explanation → teasing escalation → small sincere disclosure → abrupt exit or phone call.**

Unlike Rio, who disguises emotion behind formality, indignation, or managerial logic, Aoi often says the emotionally important thing plainly, then moves on before anyone can make it too dramatic.

---

## 1\. Aoi wants to be watched, but mostly through dance

Aoi repeatedly asks the manager to come see her, but she usually routes the desire through performance. She says she wants him to come to the dressing room, that she wants him to see more of her — then clarifies “the dance.” She says his presence backstage is reassuring, and that she trusts him. She also says that if he thinks her performance is great, that alone makes her happy. message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m…

This is the Aoi version of the Rio “watch me” motif. Rio wants to show the manager her cool side and becomes flustered by the desire. Aoi is less embarrassed about wanting his gaze, but she anchors it in dance: see me dancing, see the formation, see the work, come to the room, be nearby.

**Contribution:** core for Aoi/manager. His gaze matters because he witnesses the part of her that feels most real.

---

## 2\. Aoi’s intimacy with the manager is teasing, permissive, and strangely direct

Aoi repeatedly treats the manager as someone she can play with. She jokes about uploading a video of him sleeping, asks him to dance while giving a White Day return gift, invites herself to his house to play games, asks him to choose clothes and “make her his type,” offers to cook or make sweets, asks for his favorite foods, asks him to do dog impressions, and suggests he come as her bride in wedding-role contexts. message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m…

The important nuance is that Aoi’s flirtation often looks like play before it looks like romance. She does not usually become Rio-style flustered. Instead, she creates a situation where the manager must react, and then she observes the reaction. Her affection is experimental: “what happens if I say this?” “what happens if I ask him this?” “what happens if I make him bark like a dog?”

**Contribution:** core for private Aoi. She experiences closeness as permission to be troublesome.

---

## 3\. Aoi is more aware of “being a girl” than her neutral style suggests

Several messages show Aoi noticing gendered expectations and bodily insecurity. She comments that she is still a年頃の女の子 / girl of that age, even if the manager may not think of her that way. She worries about body shape, chest growth, swimsuit demand, diet, and whether she has an “乙女” side. She also receives lots of Valentine chocolate from girls at school and says she does not care much about age or gender in how she treats people. message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m…

This is important because Aoi’s **僕** register, sporty behavior, and role-reversal humor can make her seem ungendered or detached from femininity. The messages complicate that. She is not unaware of being female; she just refuses to organize her entire self around expected feminine presentation. She can diet, worry, tease, wear swimsuits, play groom, make sweets, and still remain unmistakably Aoi.

**Contribution:** core for Aoi’s gender presentation. She resists passive femininity, not femininity itself.

---

## 4\. Aoi’s bond with Rio remains private, domestic, and teasingly possessive

The messages constantly reinforce the Rio/Aoi old-partner layer. Aoi references eating pancakes with Rio, cooking with Rio, Rio making high-quality coffee for her birthday, Rio nagging her about fashion, Rio’s anger over loose habits, Rio’s low boiling point, Rio’s old “mad dog” resemblance, and Rio’s hidden cute side. She also uses the “secret my Lily” message to summon the manager to help consume the results of a Rio pancake overproduction incident. message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m…

The line of continuity from the prior card stories is strong: Rio is not “just another member” in Aoi’s messages. Rio is a recurring domestic presence. Aoi complains about her, relies on her, teases her, watches her, defends her, and treats her hidden behaviors as valuable insider knowledge.

Aoi also says that the old two-person LizNoir had a comfortable silence. That is a crucial detail: Rio and Aoi did not need constant talk to feel connected. Their bond was not chatty intimacy but **shared air**. message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m…

**Contribution:** foundational for Rio/Aoi. The messages strengthen the reading that their intimacy is old, domestic, bodily, private, and very hard to replace.

---

## 5\. Aoi is slowly learning communication as a senior

The bond stories and messages repeatedly show Aoi becoming more senior-like. She trains Ai, worries when Ai fails, asks the manager to comfort Ai, coaches Kotono and Rei, notices Kokoro and Ai’s growth, thinks about how to teach dance, and realizes that conversation before performance can help juniors relax. bond\_aoi\_001\_aoi message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m…

The important point is that Aoi does not naturally teach through explanation. She teaches through doing. That makes her powerful but sometimes difficult as a senior. Rio even identifies “teaching” as Aoi’s課題 / assignment. Aoi accepts this reluctantly, but seriously.

**Contribution:** core for mature Aoi. She is no longer just the person Rio manages; she is becoming someone who manages growth in others.

---

## 6\. Aoi’s performance philosophy remains “dance first,” but it broadens

Aoi still says things like: she did not become an idol to talk, she would rather dance than do MC, fans should just watch lives, and she wants to do more dance-centered work. But the message layer shows this starting to broaden. She learns that fan letters reveal parts of herself she did not notice, that fan events have their own meaning, that MC has “物好き” who enjoy it, that social-media posts can be idol activity, and that foreign fans require language study. message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m…

This is a major Aoi growth line. She does not abandon “dance first.” Instead, she adds supporting systems around dance: language, MC, fan response, SNS, coaching, styling, interviews, and outside work.

**Contribution:** core for idol-professional Aoi. Her artistry remains bodily, but her idea of idol work becomes less narrow.

---

## 7\. Aoi’s identity anxiety is not about fame itself, but about becoming “not Aoi”

The message-story arc **“楽しく踊りたいだけなのに”** is especially important. Aoi says that as LizNoir becomes more popular, she is recognized more often in town, and although popularity is good, she feels uneasy because she did not aim for that. She says she just wants to dance happily, and later clarifies that she feels like she is becoming less herself in exchange for popularity. The manager helps by arranging studio access so she can dance at night in a controlled place. message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m…

This is a key Aoi anxiety. She is not afraid of attention exactly; she is afraid that fame will change the conditions under which she can move freely. Her solution is not to reject popularity but to secure places where she can remain Aoi.

**Contribution:** foundational for Aoi. This arc states the core tension of her career: visibility threatens freedom, but proper support can make visibility livable.

---

## 8\. Aoi’s relationship with fans is understated but real

Aoi sometimes says fans should just watch lives, worries that fan events reduce live time, and does not always know what to do with fanservice. But she also reads fan letters, is moved that fans notice good things she herself does not see, and becomes so energized that she wants to dance for five or six hours afterward. message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m…

That is a very Aoi fan relationship: not gushy, not theatrical, but catalytic. Fans make her want to move. Their words become physical energy.

**Contribution:** important for Aoi’s idol identity. She may resist idol-social conventions, but she is not indifferent to fans.

---

## 9\. Aoi’s weaknesses are concrete and funny: dogs, crab sticks, phones, boredom, and social logistics

Aoi’s recurring dislikes — dogs and crab sticks — are not just profile trivia. They structure multiple message arcs. She panics about animal programs, worries about dog videos, asks the manager for workarounds, and partially copes by using his dog imitation as a mental buffer. She also repeatedly mishandles phones, ignores messages, turns her phone off, or breaks it through physical movement. message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m…

These weaknesses keep her from being a perfect cool genius. She is formidable onstage, but mundane systems defeat her: dogs, communications etiquette, reporting, school procedures, fashion maintenance, and boredom.

**Contribution:** important for humanizing Aoi. Her limitations are not contradictions; they reveal that her competence is sharply concentrated around movement and embodied expression.

---

# Highest-value message arcs for future Aoi synthesis

The highest-priority message material is:

**“楽しく踊りたいだけなのに”** — Aoi feels fame making her “not herself,” and the manager creates night studio access. This is foundational for freedom vs visibility. message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m…

**“歌のレッスン”** — Aoi struggles with vocal training because dance comes naturally and song feels less intuitive; later, Rio praises her improvement. This is important for Aoi as a performer beyond dance. message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m…

**“沸点が低い”** — criticism of Aoi’s dance prompts Rio, Ai, and Kokoro to push Aoi forward; Aoi notes everyone else’s boiling point is low, but then performs and accepts that she needs to keep growing. Important for group defense of Aoi and Rio’s pride in her. message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m…

**“カニカマと犬” / “いい声で鳴くんだね”** — Aoi’s dog fear becomes both comedy and dependence on the manager. Important for vulnerability and trust. message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m…

**“お兄ちゃん君”** — Aoi reflects on siblings, age, gender, and forms of address; she says she does not care much about age or gender and experiments with names. Very important for her social ontology: categories are flexible if the relationship feels right. message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m…

**Rio-related instant messages** — pancake outings, Rio nagging, Rio’s hidden cute side, old comfortable silence, formation praise, and Aoi’s desire for the manager to praise Rio too. These are essential for Rio/Aoi synthesis. message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_aoi\_001\_m…

---

# Overall synthesis: what this batch adds to Aoi

This batch turns Aoi into a fully lived-in character.

She is still the free genius dancer, but the everyday layer shows that freedom has many small habits:

She trains during lunch because movement will not wait.
She turns boredom into wall-jump drills.
She gives homemade sweets and then pretends it is not a big deal.
She recognizes juniors’ talent but struggles to teach gently.
She wants praise, but often turns it into a joke.
She dislikes fame when it interferes with free dancing.
She worries about being unfriendly, unfeminine, or not idol-like enough.
She accepts fans’ love most easily when it makes her want to dance.
She treats Rio’s hidden softness as private treasure.
She treats the manager as someone she can bother, trust, tease, and ask to stay nearby.
She is cool until a dog appears.
She is mature until paperwork, school, or communications discipline appears.
She is unserious until the subject is dance, LizNoir, Rio, or someone’s growth.

The refined thesis after this batch:

**Igawa Aoi is a dancer whose body understands the world faster than her social language does. Her charm lies in the gap between her effortless physical genius and her awkward, deadpan attempts to process everything else: fame, fanservice, school, femininity, juniors, phones, dogs, gifts, romance, and ordinary companionship. She grows not by becoming less free, but by learning where her freedom needs translation so that other people can stand beside her.**

And for Rio’s eventual synthesis, the key Aoi contribution is:

**Aoi is the person who keeps Rio’s pride breathable. Rio gives LizNoir weight, law, dignity, and ambition; Aoi gives it motion, mischief, silence, and the reminder that even a black-lily battle unit must still enjoy dancing.**

**Assistant — Turn 42**

These events are a strong “Aoi in motion” set. A few are duplicates of earlier Rio/LizNoir material, but they become more revealing when read from Aoi’s side.

The central update is:

**Aoi’s freedom is not just personal temperament. It is a test she applies to idolhood itself. She is drawn to anything that feels alive — summer experience, raw underground performance, Snow Miku’s impossible mobility, commercial work that either does or does not preserve LizNoir’s truth, and a new song that can only succeed when she stops thinking and starts dancing.**

Across these events, Aoi becomes less “the genius beside Rio” and more **the character who asks whether performance still has a pulse.**

# 1\. 熱中☆ハプニングサマー — Aoi learns that “experience” is part of performance

IMPORTANT\_011\_\_event\_2022\_008\_s…

This event is light in tone but very useful for Aoi because it exposes both her strengths and her limits.

At the start, Aoi and Rio are almost comically over-professionalized. Ai and Kokoro say they want to spend the off day with their seniors, and Rio/Aoi interpret that as “let’s train.” When Ai asks how the seniors can keep practicing after a live, Aoi answers as if it is obvious: **she dances every day, so whether there was a live yesterday does not matter.** That single beat captures Aoi’s bodily worldview. Dance is not “work” first; it is maintenance, appetite, metabolism. IMPORTANT\_011\_\_event\_2022\_008\_s…

But the story also shows Aoi’s limitation: because dance is so natural to her, she does not immediately understand that the juniors wanted **play**, not just more LizNoir discipline. The Okinawa trip becomes necessary because Rio and Aoi cannot write a summer song from abstract effort. They need actual experience: sea, heat, swimsuit embarrassment, getting hit on, getting lost, cooking outdoors, accidental campfire chaos, and shared frustration.

Aoi’s specific role is subtler than Rio’s. Rio is the one who keeps trying to master “summer” as a concept. Aoi is the one who drifts between skepticism and curiosity. At first, she says the sea is the sea and Hoshimi’s beach would have been enough; later, she is the one who tells Rio they should go see the ocean anyway because they need any clue they can find. That is Aoi’s instinctive practicality: if the body/world might teach them something, go try it.

This event also contains a very useful Aoi gender/romance beat. During the campfire meal, she praises Ai’s cooking and says Ai would make a good bride, only for Rio to scold her for saying that to another girl and say Aoi is the one who would become a bride. Aoi answers that she would rather **receive/take a bride** than become one. Combined with the beach scene where Aoi fails to recognize she is being hit on, this reinforces Aoi’s recurring pattern: she is not unaware of romance or gender, but she does not instinctively place herself in the passive “girl being chosen” role. She tends to imagine affection through action, taking, protecting, feeding, or moving.

The ending matters most. After the summer song succeeds, Kokoro suggests fireworks, Rio says she has had enough “summer experience,” and Aoi pushes back: **work aside, they are precious companions, so they should play together.** Rio is surprised that Aoi says this, which is exactly why it matters. Aoi, who often treats life as dance-first, recognizes that four-member LizNoir needs memories that are not reducible to work. The final lines frame the fireworks as another “good summer memory.” IMPORTANT\_011\_\_event\_2022\_008\_s…

**Contribution to Aoi:** Important. It shows Aoi’s dance-as-default life, her blunt romance/gender-role instincts, her growing recognition that shared non-work memories can feed performance, and her role as the person who can coax Rio into play when the moment is right.

---

# 2\. 未来を彩るスノーフェスティバル — Aoi learns collaboration as extension, not dilution

IMPORTANT\_020\_\_event\_2024\_002\_s…

This event is less Aoi-centered than the others, but it sharpens her collaborative identity.

Aoi enters Hokkaido in full LizNoir-professional mode. She agrees with Rio that this is not just a trip: the previous live was high quality, but there were still points to correct, and LizNoir must not show anything shameful to local fans. At the same time, she has already researched soup curry and ramen. That combination is very Aoi: professional seriousness plus bodily/food curiosity. IMPORTANT\_020\_\_event\_2024\_002\_s…

Her Miku relationship is also very telling. Aoi knows Miku through rhythm games, which fits her perfectly: she approaches Miku not as abstract iconography but through rhythm, movement, playable music, and bodily response. When Miku appears as Snow Miku, Aoi is surprised but not intimidated. She treats the encounter as a creative opportunity.

The most important Aoi line in the event is not about Miku directly, but about **conflict**. When Rio and Kokoro start arguing over ideas, Miku worries that they are fighting. Aoi explains that this kind of clash is not about defeating the other person; it comes from the desire to make something better. That is one of Aoi’s clearest social-artistic insights. She understands LizNoir’s “喧嘩” not as hostility, but as friction that produces quality.

The final performance places Aoi inside LizNoir’s ritual language. In the group call, Ai says “限界超えても,” Kokoro says “歌を纏って,” Miku says “想いを抱いて,” Aoi says **“決して枯れない,”** and Rio completes it with “気高く咲き誇れ.” This distribution is meaningful: Aoi voices the “never wither” part of LizNoir’s identity — endurance, persistent life, the flower that remains alive through snow, travel, and collaboration. IMPORTANT\_020\_\_event\_2024\_002\_s…

**Contribution to Aoi:** Important but not individually foundational. It shows Aoi as a collaborator who can absorb an external performer/icon without losing LizNoir’s core, and as someone who understands creative conflict as a generative force.

---

# 3\. IDOLY MATCH～地下闘技場への挑戦～ — Aoi discovers idolhood outside official legitimacy

IMPORTANT\_026\_\_event\_2026\_004\_s…

This event is extremely important because it gives Aoi a second idol-world outside polished professional rankings.

Aoi secretly sneaks out at night, and the initial comedy is that everyone imagines ordinary “bad” nighttime behavior: sauna, mahjong, ramen. The truth is stranger: she has been attending the **アイドル地下闘技場**, a venue for non-official “野良アイドル” — people not registered in the VENUS Program, including those who dislike AI evaluation, have personal reasons not to belong to agencies, or perform as hobby/subculture idols. Aoi explains that these people still want to shine as idols, even outside official structures. IMPORTANT\_026\_\_event\_2026\_004\_s…

This is huge for Aoi because it reveals that her artistic curiosity is not status-bound. She is BIG4-level, but she is fascinated by a raw underground space precisely because it has something the official system can lack: **anything-goes intensity, dignity staked on performance, soul collision, and unfiltered heat.**

This is not Aoi slumming for novelty. She describes being struck like lightning by the performances. That matters because Aoi’s deepest criterion has always been: does the performance feel alive? The underground arena answers yes so strongly that she risks scandal to keep returning.

The Mei/Rei trio also matters. Outside LizNoir, Aoi becomes the instigator of a secret cross-unit performance experiment. She drags Rei and Mei into the underground world, proposes disguises, accepts ridiculous stage names, and eventually enters the tournament. This shows Aoi’s infectious quality: when she finds a living performance space, she makes other performers feel the itch to move too.

DJばぁば and BEIJU are the event’s philosophical turn. Aoi initially sees them as bizarre “strong enemies,” but in the final she realizes that technical superiority is not the whole measure. Even if Aoi/Rei/Mei have better song and dance skills, BEIJU’s stage makes the audience want to cheer. Aoi says that people who challenge what they love at any age, work hard, grow with friends, and give everything to make the audience happy are **real idols**. IMPORTANT\_026\_\_event\_2026\_004\_s…

This is one of the most important expansions of Aoi’s idol philosophy. Until now, Aoi’s core was “I want to dance.” Here, she confronts idolhood as something that can exist outside youth, official rankings, technical elite performance, and even conventional polish. The audience’s desire to cheer becomes evidence of idol truth.

**Contribution to Aoi:** Core. It broadens Aoi from elite dancer to artistic pluralist. She learns that idolhood can be underground, unregistered, old, strange, technically inferior, and still real if the performance makes people want to believe in it.

---

# 4\. すれ違いのディソナンス — Aoi as guardian of LizNoir’s essence

CORE\_023\_\_event\_2024\_005\_st-eve…

This is one of the most important Aoi events in the entire set.

For Rio, this event is about growth versus compromise. For Aoi, it is about **the line between evolution and falsification**.

The setup is the Tokyo Seaside tie-up. It is a huge visibility opportunity that could help LizNoir reach BIG4, but the manager has a bad feeling, and Aoi takes that seriously. She says she trusts his intuition. That detail matters because Aoi is not just reflexively oppositional; she is sensing misalignment before the obvious disaster arrives. CORE\_023\_\_event\_2024\_005\_st-eve…

Once the producer appears, Aoi’s alarm becomes justified. The direction is hyper-cute, generic, and painfully off-brand: bunny jumps, forced smiles, “ハッピーピカピカスマイル,” and eventually a song whose lyrics are so off that Aoi simply says the thing everyone is avoiding: they are too different from LizNoir’s past music, another idol would suit them better, and the lyrics are bad. CORE\_023\_\_event\_2024\_005\_st-eve…

The Aoi/Rio argument is the core. Rio argues that change is necessary: if LizNoir wants to grow, they must learn to express other people’s intentions, not only their own. Aoi accepts that change can matter, but rejects the idea that every external request deserves performance. Her position is:

**LizNoir can change, but not into something that anyone could do.**

Aoi’s strongest line of thinking is that if the work erases why LizNoir was chosen, then the work is false. She asks why LizNoir was hired at all if the result could be performed by a more suitable cute idol group. That is not stubbornness; it is brand ethics and artistic self-respect.

She also articulates a professional ethics that is surprisingly mature. When the producer’s sponsor-pressure situation is revealed, Aoi does not just blame him for being annoying. She says his job is not to hide behind forced high tension, but to stand between sponsor and performer, hear both sides, and coordinate. This is Aoi at her sharpest: blunt, unsentimental, but correct.

The final outcome vindicates both Rio and Aoi. Rio is right that dialogue and adaptation are necessary. Aoi is right that adaptation without essence becomes betrayal. The final commercial works because it finds a LizNoir-native form of cuteness: not generic smiles, but the natural warmth of the four members playing together after tension is resolved.

**Contribution to Aoi:** Foundational. It defines Aoi as LizNoir’s artistic conscience — the person willing to say “no” when everyone else is tempted to call compromise growth.

---

# 5\. let’s 湯けむり dancing！ — Aoi learns responsibility without losing freedom

CORE\_024\_\_event\_2025\_010\_st-eve…

This is probably the most important Aoi event in this batch.

Aoi asks to lead a new song for overseas tour additions. That alone is a major shift. She is usually the one who resists managerial responsibility unless it touches dance directly, but here she says she wants to test herself: **what does she want to do in LizNoir, and what can she do for LizNoir?** CORE\_024\_\_event\_2025\_010\_st-eve…

Her concept is very Aoi: **freedom and passion.** She wants a song that is not trapped by “LizNoir-like” expectations, because freedom itself is one of LizNoir’s strengths. She connects passion not only to 「情熱だけで生きてみろ」 but further back to the American live-house lesson: what matters is not perfectly accurate dancing, but passion that reaches the viewer’s heart. CORE\_024\_\_event\_2025\_010\_st-eve…

Then she completely spirals.

The comedy — Indian film, flamenco, hula, limbo, samba, repeated hot-spring “inspirations” — is funny because it weaponizes Aoi’s intuitive creativity against her. She keeps trying to find an external genre that will “solve” freedom and passion. But that is exactly the problem. Aoi, whose strength is normally instinct, has become trapped by responsibility. Because she is afraid of failing LizNoir, she starts thinking in concepts rather than dancing from the body.

Rio is the one who finds her. The American practice location matters: it is the place where the four-member LizNoir rediscovered passion. Rio tells Aoi that she is overthinking and should dance freely, because Aoi is most alive when she sees people dancing and wants to join. Aoi then asks Rio to dance with her, and while dancing with Rio, the choreography begins to come. CORE\_024\_\_event\_2025\_010\_st-eve…

That is the event’s emotional center. Aoi cannot think herself back into Aoi. She has to move, and she has to move with Rio.

The later realization is excellent: Aoi had been searching for a fixed form called “LizNoir-like dance,” but understands that this was backwards. There is no external shape called LizNoir-ness. **When Rio, Aoi, Ai, and Kokoro each dance as themselves together, that is LizNoir-like dance.** This is one of the cleanest mature-LizNoir formulations in the corpus.

The finale deepens her further. Before the first performance, Aoi realizes the strange physical feeling she has been having — fast heartbeat, cold fingertips, excitement mixed with anxiety — is **nervousness**. She has felt pressure because she did not want to ruin LizNoir. Rio is happy, not because Aoi suffered, but because that fear proves how deeply Aoi loves the group. The cheek-squishing ritual from the first one-man live returns, making Rio/Aoi’s private history part of Aoi’s present creative courage.

**Contribution to Aoi:** Foundational / core. It is the event where Aoi’s freedom matures into responsibility, then has to be rescued from responsibility by the very freedom that made Aoi Aoi.

---

# Cross-event synthesis

## 1\. Aoi’s core is not “dance”; it is aliveness

These events clarify that Aoi is drawn to aliveness in every form:

-   summer chaos that becomes song;

-   Snow Miku’s impossible virtual/light movement;

-   underground idols whose performances are rough but soulful;

-   LizNoir’s essence under commercial pressure;

-   samba becoming usable only when the group dances as themselves.


Aoi’s question is always: **does this move? does this breathe? does it make bodies and hearts respond?**

That is why she can respect DJばぁば, Miku, Rio, Ai, Kokoro, underground idols, and street audiences for different reasons. She is not a hierarchy snob. She is a vitality snob.

## 2\. Aoi is more professional than she looks

Aoi often sounds casual, but these events show a consistent professional spine. She checks whether a tie-up should be accepted, asks why LizNoir was chosen, refuses generic work, thinks about fans in Hokkaido, worries about whether her new song will ruin LizNoir, and recognizes that a raw underground stage can still contain idol dignity.

Her professionalism is not Rio’s polish. It is more instinctive and bodily:

**a good job should have a living reason to exist.**

## 3\. Aoi’s “freedom” becomes relational

Early Aoi freedom can look solitary: she wants to dance because dancing feels good. These events make it relational.

She wants Rio to dance with her.
She wants Ai and Kokoro to create memories.
She wants Hokkaido fans to receive something special.
She wants underground idols to be recognized as real.
She wants LizNoir’s group individuality preserved.
She wants the new song to let all four members become themselves more fully.

That is the mature Aoi arc: freedom stops meaning “I do what I want alone” and becomes “I create conditions where everyone can move truthfully.”

## 4\. Aoi is Rio’s necessary counterweight

These events are invaluable for Rio’s eventual synthesis because they show the Rio/Aoi axis under stress.

In **熱中☆ハプニングサマー**, Aoi helps Rio accept play.
In **すれ違いのディソナンス**, Aoi challenges Rio’s over-adaptive leadership.
In **let’s 湯けむり dancing！**, Rio returns the favor by restoring Aoi to instinctive movement.

Their partnership is reciprocal:

**Rio keeps Aoi responsible. Aoi keeps Rio alive.**
Rio gives LizNoir direction, standard, ambition, and nobility.
Aoi gives LizNoir motion, truth, looseness, and the courage to enjoy battle.

## 5\. Aoi’s idol philosophy becomes much broader than LizNoir

The underground event is especially important because it prevents Aoi’s worldview from being too narrow. Aoi is LizNoir through and through, but she can recognize idol truth beyond LizNoir’s style and beyond the VENUS Program. She can lose to BEIJU and accept that the audience’s response is meaningful.

That makes Aoi one of the better characters for articulating the franchise’s broad thesis of idolhood: an idol is not merely a ranked professional, a technically superior performer, or a polished product. An idol is someone whose performance makes others want to watch, cheer, believe, and come back.

---

# Importance ranking for Aoi

**Foundational / core**

-   **let’s 湯けむり dancing！** — Aoi’s responsibility crisis, freedom/passion thesis, Rio/Aoi partnership, and “LizNoir-like = us dancing as ourselves.” CORE\_024\_\_event\_2025\_010\_st-eve…

-   **すれ違いのディソナンス** — Aoi as guardian of LizNoir’s essence against false commercial compromise. CORE\_023\_\_event\_2024\_005\_st-eve…

-   **IDOLY MATCH～地下闘技場への挑戦～** — Aoi’s expanded idol philosophy: raw, unofficial, old, strange, and technically imperfect performances can still be “real idol” performances. IMPORTANT\_026\_\_event\_2026\_004\_s…


**Important**

-   **熱中☆ハプニングサマー** — Aoi learns that shared experience and play can matter as much as training; also important for her role-reversal romance/gender coding and four-member warmth. IMPORTANT\_011\_\_event\_2022\_008\_s…

-   **未来を彩るスノーフェスティバル** — Aoi as collaborator and creative-conflict interpreter; important for her Miku/rhythm-game connection and LizNoir’s ability to collaborate without dilution. IMPORTANT\_020\_\_event\_2024\_002\_s…


---

# Refined Aoi formulation after these events

**Igawa Aoi is LizNoir’s living pulse: a dancer who experiences truth through movement before language, and who judges idol work by whether it still feels alive. Her freedom is not irresponsibility; it is an artistic standard. She can accept collaboration, commercial work, underground stages, regional fan gratitude, senior responsibility, and new song leadership, but only when those things preserve the feeling that performance is breathing.**

For Rio’s eventual synthesis, the key addition is:

**Aoi is the partner who prevents Rio’s pride from hardening into mere control. She challenges Rio when Rio mistakes compromise for growth, follows Rio when Rio restores her to instinct, and helps LizNoir remain what it is at its best: not just elegant black-lily battle, but battle that still wants to dance.**

**Assistant — Turn 44**
